        Idea:
# Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment

We are migrating Reigh's app-active AI generation paths from direct Wan2GP execution to VibeComfy-backed pure-Python workflow templates. The user wants absolute feature parity with the Wan2GP path, actual RunPod validation, and tooling that prevents future agents/developers from rediscovering the same problems manually.

Current reality:
- `reigh-worker` branch: `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-control-rail-travel-matrix`
- `vibecomfy` branch: `megaplan/production-parity-templates`
- `runpod-lifecycle` branch: `megaplan/prebuilt-validation-env`
- Existing prebuilt RunPod validation volume has already proven useful but is not yet a clean, repeatable developer experience.
- Fresh installs repeatedly hit slow or flaky setup problems: network-volume stale file handles, missing system packages such as `ffmpeg`, missing custom-node assets, missing model directories in `extra_model_paths.yaml`, optional attention backends such as SageAttention, and pod lifecycle sprawl.
- A recent local fix in `reigh-worker` moved fresh/update VibeComfy virtualenvs from `/workspace/.../.venv` to `/opt/reigh-vibecomfy-live-test-venv` so package installs happen on local disk instead of the RunPod network volume. That was tested with 152 local tests and pushed.

Goal:
Build and validate a robust, reusable prebuilt environment path for Reigh VibeComfy validation. It should make the rest of the parity work easier and safer, not add a rigid second source of truth.

The target design should:
1. Provide a standard prebuilt environment for 4090-class RunPod validation.
   - Prefer RTX 4090 as the normal target because that is the user's realistic production/developer bar.
   - Keep the design able to run on larger GPUs when explicitly requested.
   - Make the environment portable when advanced kernels are unavailable, while still allowing a SageAttention-capable profile if it can be installed and validated cleanly.
2. Keep the VibeComfy workflow source pure Python.
   - No app-active workflow should rely on executing JSON blobs as the workflow implementation.
   - JSON may exist only as reference/corpus/import material, not the runtime source of truth.
3. Make environment dependencies explicit but not burdensome.
   - Capture system packages, Python version, CUDA/Torch profile, ComfyUI/VibeComfy commits, custom node lockfile state, extra model paths, optional acceleration profile, and model asset expectations in one generated or easily maintained manifest.
   - Avoid forcing developers to update a fragile hand-written manifest every time a workflow changes. Prefer deriving information from templates, contracts, lockfiles, and validation output.
4. Make validation fail early.
   - Before running expensive workflows, check the environment can import VibeComfy, restore/list custom nodes, read required model paths, validate node input schemas against the installed node classes, and resolve model assets.
   - The onboarding/check path should catch missing required connections and missing required node inputs before RunPod execution.
   - If a model asset is missing, the tool should report the exact expected asset and where it looked. If a URL is known, it should be able to reconcile/fetch or print a precise remediation.
5. Make pod lifecycle safe.
   - Never kill unrelated user pods.
   - Tag/label/name pods launched by this tooling clearly.
   - Provide a safe cleanup/status command that only targets pods launched for this validation flow.
6. Integrate with current Reigh live-test tools instead of creating a parallel universe.
   - Existing code lives primarily in `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test`, `runpod-lifecycle`, and `vibecomfy`.
   - Use the existing live-test matrix and reports.
   - Prebuilt should become the easy/default validation path when the volume/profile exists; fresh should remain a fallback and a way to validate bootstrap correctness.
7. Be documented for agents and developers.
   - Update the relevant README/skill/docs so a future agent knows when to use the prebuilt path, how to build/update it, how to validate it, and what evidence counts.
   - Document the "workflow onboarding" checklist: derive/convert/fork Python workflow, validate node schemas, validate assets, run cheap env checks, run smoke, run matrix, record contract evidence.

Expected implementation shape:
- Improve or add CLIs/scripts around prebuilt env build/check/update/reconcile/status/cleanup.
- Add tests for the new behavior.
- Where possible, use existing abstractions in VibeComfy: template registry, model asset extraction, node lock restore, node schema inspection, workflow validation/doctor/converter tools.
- If missing abstractions are found, add the smallest useful abstraction that removes repeated manual friction.
- Keep changes scoped and avoid unrelated refactors.

Validation requirements:
- Local unit tests for any changed Python modules.
- At least one remote RunPod prebuilt environment health check.
- At least one VibeComfy workflow smoke run using the prebuilt environment.
- Evidence should be written to the existing live-test report structure or a similarly discoverable report path.
- Pods launched for validation must be terminated at the end unless intentionally preserved and clearly documented.

Known useful references already produced:
- Prebuilt volume that previously passed image/edit/upscale batch: `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` (`h6ln28obus`) in `EUR-NO-1`.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T013030Z/report.json` passed 8/8 image/edit/upscale cases.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T012010Z/report.json` passed `z_image_turbo`.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260512T204040Z/report.json` passed LTX control matrix 4/4.
- Do not assume those are enough; this task is about making the prebuilt environment path systematic and then using it.

Important safety:
- Do not print secrets from `.env`, process environments, or SSH `ps e` output.
- Do not kill unrelated pods. Only clean up pods clearly launched by this flow.
- Preserve dirty user changes unless they are directly part of this work.

Success criteria:
- The repo has a clear, tested prebuilt-environment flow that future agents can discover and use by default for VibeComfy validation.
- The flow makes it obvious whether a workflow is blocked by environment setup, missing assets, invalid node inputs, or runtime workflow logic.
- Documentation explains both the "why" and the commands succinctly.
- The tooling is actually battle-tested by running at least one prebuilt RunPod health/smoke validation and recording the result.

        Requirements:
        - If the engineering brief suggests an approach, use it as your starting hypothesis — but before committing, consider if there's a simpler or more fundamental fix. The brief is well-researched input, not a final answer.
        - If the brief is absent, incomplete, or says "skip", inspect the repository yourself before planning.
        - Stay focused on the requested idea. If repo exploration surfaces unrelated issues or docs, ignore them and return to the task.
        - Prefer source code, tests, and directly relevant config files. Avoid `.megaplan/`, prior plan artifacts, and unrelated `docs/` or ops/deployment material unless the task explicitly depends on them.
        - Stop exploring once you have enough evidence to name the concrete touch points and validation path. Do not keep browsing after you can write the plan.
        - Produce a concrete implementation plan in markdown.
        - Define observable success criteria as objects with `criterion` (string) and `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`):
          - `must` — hard gate. The reviewer will block on failure. Use for correctness, functional requirements, and verifiable outcomes (e.g., "all existing tests pass", "API returns 200 for valid input"). Every `must` criterion must have a clear yes/no answer.
          - `should` — quality target. The reviewer flags but does not block. Use for subjective goals, numeric guidelines, and best-effort improvements (e.g., "file under ~300 lines", "no deeply nested conditionals", "each function has a single responsibility").
          - `info` — documented for humans, reviewer skips. Use for criteria that cannot be verified in this pipeline (e.g., "13 manual smoke tests pass", "stakeholder sign-off obtained").
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Use the `questions` field for ambiguities that would materially change implementation.
        - Use the `assumptions` field for defaults you are making so planning can proceed now.
        - Prefer cheap validation steps early.
        - Keep the plan proportional to the task. A 1-line fix needs a 2-step plan (apply fix + run tests), not a 5-step investigation.
        - If user notes answer earlier questions, incorporate them into the draft plan instead of re-asking them.
        - Fix the problem fully. Do not limit scope just to avoid breaking existing tests — update the tests too if needed.
        - Prefer the simplest, most direct fix. No fallbacks, type conversions, or defensive wrappers without concrete evidence they are needed.
        - If the task or issue hints suggest a specific approach, follow it. Only deviate with concrete counter-evidence.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

{"plan":"# Implementation Plan: Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment\n\n## Overview\nThe repository already has the right foundation rather than needing a new parallel tool: `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py` defines the manifest/contract and extracted-env probes, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py` exposes `rl prebuilt build/inspect/invalidate/list`, and `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py` consumes the volume through `--variant prebuilt` and `--variant auto`. VibeComfy already provides useful primitives in `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/commands/doctor.py`, `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/schema/validate.py`, `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/model_assets.py`, node lock restore/install code, and ready-template/porting helpers.\n\nThe simplest strong fix is to extend the existing prebuilt builder/consumer flow with a generated health manifest and reusable env-check command, then wire that check into the live-test prebuilt path before any expensive matrix run. Avoid a second source of truth: derive workflow/model/node expectations from VibeComfy templates, contracts, lockfiles, and validation output.\n\n## Phase 1: Inventory And Manifest Contract\n\n### Step 1: Extend manifest data model (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. Add a versioned `PrebuiltHealthReport` or equivalent dataclass next to `PrebuiltManifest` that records system packages, Python/CUDA/Torch profile, ComfyUI/VibeComfy commits, custom node lock hash, extra model paths, attention profile, required VibeComfy assets, model asset index summary, and validation command results.\n2. Keep `PrebuiltManifest` as the bundle compatibility contract; do not overload hard-fail fields with workflow-specific details.\n3. Add JSON read/write helpers for `env.health.json` beside `env.manifest.json`, preserving the existing atomic write style.\n\n### Step 2: Derive expected assets instead of hand-maintaining them (`vibecomfy/vibecomfy/...`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. Use VibeComfy metadata/model asset extraction paths already present in `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/commands/doctor.py` and related `model_assets` helpers as the source of model expectations.\n2. If VibeComfy lacks a clean library function for “all app-active template asset requirements”, add the smallest reusable function there and call it from RunPod tooling.\n3. Report each missing model with asset name, category/path, all paths checked under `extra_model_paths.yaml`, and URL/remediation when known.\n\n## Phase 2: Early Health Checks\n\n### Step 3: Add reusable prebuilt environment check (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)\n**Scope:** Large\n1. Add `rl prebuilt check --volume-name ... --data-center ...` that provisions a short-lived tagged probe pod, attaches the volume, extracts or inspects the prebuilt env as needed, runs the health probes, writes `env.health.json`, prints a concise result, and terminates the probe pod by default.\n2. Include checks for mount readability, required system packages (`ffmpeg`, `zstd`, `pv`), VibeComfy import, ComfyUI import, Torch CUDA version, `custom_nodes.lock` restore/list sanity, schema provider availability, `extra_model_paths.yaml`, and asset resolution.\n3. Add `--json`, `--keep-pod`, and `--attention-profile portable|sage`; default to portable RTX 4090 behavior, with larger GPU only when explicitly requested.\n\n### Step 4: Validate workflow schemas before expensive live runs (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. After bundle extraction and before `launch_worker`, call the new health check or shared probe function against the selected live-test cases.\n2. Validate that VibeComfy-supported route templates load as Python workflows, compile, pass installed-node schema checks, and have required inputs/connections.\n3. Fail with a grouped diagnostic: environment setup, missing assets, invalid node schema/input, or runtime-only logic.\n\n### Step 5: Write evidence into live-test reports (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/report.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`)\n**Scope:** Small\n1. Extend the report payload to include prebuilt volume id/name, manifest path/hash, health report path/hash, attention profile, GPU type, and check summary.\n2. Keep the existing `scripts/live_test/runs/<timestamp>/report.json` structure discoverable; add fields rather than inventing a separate report tree.\n\n## Phase 3: Safe Pod Lifecycle Commands\n\n### Step 6: Add prebuilt status and cleanup verbs (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/guard.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. Add `rl prebuilt status` that lists only pods matching known prebuilt prefixes such as `reigh-livetest-builder-` and `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-`, including age, status, GPU, cost, and whether a known volume is attached.\n2. Add `rl prebuilt cleanup --older-than ... --dry-run --yes` that only targets those prefixes and never touches arbitrary RunPod pods.\n3. Reuse existing prefix-pruning guard logic where possible, but keep the command-specific allowlist explicit and tested.\n\n## Phase 4: Tests And Docs\n\n### Step 7: Add focused unit tests (`runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_cli.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_variant_prebuilt.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1.         Idea:
# Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment

We are migrating Reigh's app-active AI generation paths from direct Wan2GP execution to VibeComfy-backed pure-Python workflow templates. The user wants absolute feature parity with the Wan2GP path, actual RunPod validation, and tooling that prevents future agents/developers from rediscovering the same problems manually.

Current reality:
- `reigh-worker` branch: `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-control-rail-travel-matrix`
- `vibecomfy` branch: `megaplan/production-parity-templates`
- `runpod-lifecycle` branch: `megaplan/prebuilt-validation-env`
- Existing prebuilt RunPod validation volume has already proven useful but is not yet a clean, repeatable developer experience.
- Fresh installs repeatedly hit slow or flaky setup problems: network-volume stale file handles, missing system packages such as `ffmpeg`, missing custom-node assets, missing model directories in `extra_model_paths.yaml`, optional attention backends such as SageAttention, and pod lifecycle sprawl.
- A recent local fix in `reigh-worker` moved fresh/update VibeComfy virtualenvs from `/workspace/.../.venv` to `/opt/reigh-vibecomfy-live-test-venv` so package installs happen on local disk instead of the RunPod network volume. That was tested with 152 local tests and pushed.

Goal:
Build and validate a robust, reusable prebuilt environment path for Reigh VibeComfy validation. It should make the rest of the parity work easier and safer, not add a rigid second source of truth.

The target design should:
1. Provide a standard prebuilt environment for 4090-class RunPod validation.
   - Prefer RTX 4090 as the normal target because that is the user's realistic production/developer bar.
   - Keep the design able to run on larger GPUs when explicitly requested.
   - Make the environment portable when advanced kernels are unavailable, while still allowing a SageAttention-capable profile if it can be installed and validated cleanly.
2. Keep the VibeComfy workflow source pure Python.
   - No app-active workflow should rely on executing JSON blobs as the workflow implementation.
   - JSON may exist only as reference/corpus/import material, not the runtime source of truth.
3. Make environment dependencies explicit but not burdensome.
   - Capture system packages, Python version, CUDA/Torch profile, ComfyUI/VibeComfy commits, custom node lockfile state, extra model paths, optional acceleration profile, and model asset expectations in one generated or easily maintained manifest.
   - Avoid forcing developers to update a fragile hand-written manifest every time a workflow changes. Prefer deriving information from templates, contracts, lockfiles, and validation output.
4. Make validation fail early.
   - Before running expensive workflows, check the environment can import VibeComfy, restore/list custom nodes, read required model paths, validate node input schemas against the installed node classes, and resolve model assets.
   - The onboarding/check path should catch missing required connections and missing required node inputs before RunPod execution.
   - If a model asset is missing, the tool should report the exact expected asset and where it looked. If a URL is known, it should be able to reconcile/fetch or print a precise remediation.
5. Make pod lifecycle safe.
   - Never kill unrelated user pods.
   - Tag/label/name pods launched by this tooling clearly.
   - Provide a safe cleanup/status command that only targets pods launched for this validation flow.
6. Integrate with current Reigh live-test tools instead of creating a parallel universe.
   - Existing code lives primarily in `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test`, `runpod-lifecycle`, and `vibecomfy`.
   - Use the existing live-test matrix and reports.
   - Prebuilt should become the easy/default validation path when the volume/profile exists; fresh should remain a fallback and a way to validate bootstrap correctness.
7. Be documented for agents and developers.
   - Update the relevant README/skill/docs so a future agent knows when to use the prebuilt path, how to build/update it, how to validate it, and what evidence counts.
   - Document the "workflow onboarding" checklist: derive/convert/fork Python workflow, validate node schemas, validate assets, run cheap env checks, run smoke, run matrix, record contract evidence.

Expected implementation shape:
- Improve or add CLIs/scripts around prebuilt env build/check/update/reconcile/status/cleanup.
- Add tests for the new behavior.
- Where possible, use existing abstractions in VibeComfy: template registry, model asset extraction, node lock restore, node schema inspection, workflow validation/doctor/converter tools.
- If missing abstractions are found, add the smallest useful abstraction that removes repeated manual friction.
- Keep changes scoped and avoid unrelated refactors.

Validation requirements:
- Local unit tests for any changed Python modules.
- At least one remote RunPod prebuilt environment health check.
- At least one VibeComfy workflow smoke run using the prebuilt environment.
- Evidence should be written to the existing live-test report structure or a similarly discoverable report path.
- Pods launched for validation must be terminated at the end unless intentionally preserved and clearly documented.

Known useful references already produced:
- Prebuilt volume that previously passed image/edit/upscale batch: `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` (`h6ln28obus`) in `EUR-NO-1`.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T013030Z/report.json` passed 8/8 image/edit/upscale cases.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T012010Z/report.json` passed `z_image_turbo`.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260512T204040Z/report.json` passed LTX control matrix 4/4.
- Do not assume those are enough; this task is about making the prebuilt environment path systematic and then using it.

Important safety:
- Do not print secrets from `.env`, process environments, or SSH `ps e` output.
- Do not kill unrelated pods. Only clean up pods clearly launched by this flow.
- Preserve dirty user changes unless they are directly part of this work.

Success criteria:
- The repo has a clear, tested prebuilt-environment flow that future agents can discover and use by default for VibeComfy validation.
- The flow makes it obvious whether a workflow is blocked by environment setup, missing assets, invalid node inputs, or runtime workflow logic.
- Documentation explains both the "why" and the commands succinctly.
- The tooling is actually battle-tested by running at least one prebuilt RunPod health/smoke validation and recording the result.

User notes and answers:
- Resolved plan questions: (1) do not hard-code eu-no-1/eur-no-1; derive canonical prebuilt volume names from the actual RunPod data_center_id normalization and have docs mention the observed reference volume only as an example. (2) Required first remote smoke gate should be cheapest z_image_turbo, then broader image/edit/upscale or video matrix runs after base health is proven. (3) SageAttention should be an optional validated profile: implement/install/check support when requested, but portable 4090 validation must remain the default and must not be blocked by Sage installation failure unless the user explicitly requests the sage profile.

        Current plan (markdown):
        # Implementation Plan: Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment

## Overview
The repository already has the right foundation rather than needing a new parallel tool: `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py` defines the manifest/contract and extracted-env probes, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py` exposes `rl prebuilt build/inspect/invalidate/list`, and `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py` consumes the volume through `--variant prebuilt` and `--variant auto`. VibeComfy already provides useful primitives in `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/commands/doctor.py`, `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/schema/validate.py`, `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/model_assets.py`, node lock restore/install code, and ready-template/porting helpers.

The simplest strong fix is to extend the existing prebuilt builder/consumer flow with a generated health manifest and reusable env-check command, then wire that check into the live-test prebuilt path before any expensive matrix run. Avoid a second source of truth: derive workflow/model/node expectations from VibeComfy templates, contracts, lockfiles, and validation output.

## Phase 1: Inventory And Manifest Contract

### Step 1: Extend manifest data model (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a versioned `PrebuiltHealthReport` or equivalent dataclass next to `PrebuiltManifest` that records system packages, Python/CUDA/Torch profile, ComfyUI/VibeComfy commits, custom node lock hash, extra model paths, attention profile, required VibeComfy assets, model asset index summary, and validation command results.
2. Keep `PrebuiltManifest` as the bundle compatibility contract; do not overload hard-fail fields with workflow-specific details.
3. Add JSON read/write helpers for `env.health.json` beside `env.manifest.json`, preserving the existing atomic write style.

### Step 2: Derive expected assets instead of hand-maintaining them (`vibecomfy/vibecomfy/...`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Use VibeComfy metadata/model asset extraction paths already present in `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/commands/doctor.py` and related `model_assets` helpers as the source of model expectations.
2. If VibeComfy lacks a clean library function for “all app-active template asset requirements”, add the smallest reusable function there and call it from RunPod tooling.
3. Report each missing model with asset name, category/path, all paths checked under `extra_model_paths.yaml`, and URL/remediation when known.

## Phase 2: Early Health Checks

### Step 3: Add reusable prebuilt environment check (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Add `rl prebuilt check --volume-name ... --data-center ...` that provisions a short-lived tagged probe pod, attaches the volume, extracts or inspects the prebuilt env as needed, runs the health probes, writes `env.health.json`, prints a concise result, and terminates the probe pod by default.
2. Include checks for mount readability, required system packages (`ffmpeg`, `zstd`, `pv`), VibeComfy import, ComfyUI import, Torch CUDA version, `custom_nodes.lock` restore/list sanity, schema provider availability, `extra_model_paths.yaml`, and asset resolution.
3. Add `--json`, `--keep-pod`, and `--attention-profile portable|sage`; default to portable RTX 4090 behavior, with larger GPU only when explicitly requested.

### Step 4: Validate workflow schemas before expensive live runs (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. After bundle extraction and before `launch_worker`, call the new health check or shared probe function against the selected live-test cases.
2. Validate that VibeComfy-supported route templates load as Python workflows, compile, pass installed-node schema checks, and have required inputs/connections.
3. Fail with a grouped diagnostic: environment setup, missing assets, invalid node schema/input, or runtime-only logic.

### Step 5: Write evidence into live-test reports (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/report.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. Extend the report payload to include prebuilt volume id/name, manifest path/hash, health report path/hash, attention profile, GPU type, and check summary.
2. Keep the existing `scripts/live_test/runs/<timestamp>/report.json` structure discoverable; add fields rather than inventing a separate report tree.

## Phase 3: Safe Pod Lifecycle Commands

### Step 6: Add prebuilt status and cleanup verbs (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/guard.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `rl prebuilt status` that lists only pods matching known prebuilt prefixes such as `reigh-livetest-builder-` and `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-`, including age, status, GPU, cost, and whether a known volume is attached.
2. Add `rl prebuilt cleanup --older-than ... --dry-run --yes` that only targets those prefixes and never touches arbitrary RunPod pods.
3. Reuse existing prefix-pruning guard logic where possible, but keep the command-specific allowlist explicit and tested.

## Phase 4: Tests And Docs

### Step 7: Add focused unit tests (`runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_cli.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_variant_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Test manifest/health JSON round trip, missing asset diagnostics, schema-check failure formatting, status/cleanup prefix filtering, `--keep-pod` behavior, and default termination.
2. Add dry-run tests for `rl prebuilt check/status/cleanup` that require no RunPod credentials.
3. Add a prebuilt consumer test proving expensive matrix queueing is skipped when health checks fail.

### Step 8: Document the default workflow (`runpod-lifecycle/README.md`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/README.md` or nearest existing doc, VibeComfy docs if present)
**Scope:** Small
1. Document when to use prebuilt vs fresh, how to build/check/update/invalidate/status/cleanup, and what evidence counts.
2. Add the workflow onboarding checklist: fork/derive Python workflow, run schema validation, verify model assets, run cheap prebuilt check, run smoke, run matrix, record report.
3. State that app-active workflows must be pure Python and JSON is reference/import material only.

## Phase 5: Remote Validation

### Step 9: Run local verification first
**Scope:** Small
1. Run targeted tests first: `pytest runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_cli.py` and `pytest reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_variant_prebuilt.py`.
2. Run the broader affected suites if targeted tests pass.

### Step 10: Battle-test on RunPod
**Scope:** Large
1. Run `rl prebuilt check` against the known portable volume `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` / `h6ln28obus` in `EUR-NO-1`, or the corrected current volume name if discovery shows it differs.
2. Run one cheap VibeComfy smoke through `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py --variant prebuilt --backend vibecomfy --case z_image_turbo` using the generated health report.
3. Confirm the launched pod is terminated unless `--keep-pod` is intentionally used, and record the report path in the final implementation notes.

## Execution Order
1. Add health-report data structures and local derivation helpers before CLI behavior.
2. Implement `rl prebuilt check` and tests before wiring it into live-test execution.
3. Add status/cleanup lifecycle safety before running remote validation.
4. Update docs after commands and report paths are stable.
5. Run local tests, then one remote health check, then one remote smoke.

## Validation Order
1. Static/dry-run checks for new CLI args and parsing.
2. Unit tests for manifest, health report, diagnostics, and cleanup filters.
3. Prebuilt live-test dry run to confirm command shape and report wiring.
4. Real RunPod `prebuilt check`.
5. Real prebuilt VibeComfy smoke run with report evidence and pod cleanup confirmation.

  "robustness": "standard",
  "signals": {
    "iteration": 1,
    "idea": "# Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment\n\nWe are migrating Reigh's app-active AI generation paths from direct Wan2GP execution to VibeComfy-backed pure-Python workflow templates. The user wants absolute feature parity with the Wan2GP path, actual RunPod validation, and tooling that prevents future agents/developers from rediscovering the same problems manually.\n\nCurrent reality:\n- `reigh-worker` branch: `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-control-rail-travel-matrix`\n- `vibecomfy` branch: `megaplan/production-parity-templates`\n- `runpod-lifecycle` branch: `megaplan/prebuilt-validation-env`\n- Existing prebuilt RunPod validation volume has already proven useful but is not yet a clean, repeatable developer experience.\n- Fresh installs repeatedly hit slow or flaky setup problems: network-volume stale file handles, missing system packages such as `ffmpeg`, missing custom-node assets, missing model directories in `extra_model_paths.yaml`, optional attention backends such as SageAttention, and pod lifecycle sprawl.\n- A recent local fix in `reigh-worker` moved fresh/update VibeComfy virtualenvs from `/workspace/.../.venv` to `/opt/reigh-vibecomfy-live-test-venv` so package installs happen on local disk instead of the RunPod network volume. That was tested with 152 local tests and pushed.\n\nGoal:\nBuild and validate a robust, reusable prebuilt environment path for Reigh VibeComfy validation. It should make the rest of the parity work easier and safer, not add a rigid second source of truth.\n\nThe target design should:\n1. Provide a standard prebuilt environment for 4090-class RunPod validation.\n   - Prefer RTX 4090 as the normal target because that is the user's realistic production/developer bar.\n   - Keep the design able to run on larger GPUs when explicitly requested.\n   - Make the environment portable when advanced kernels are unavailable, while still allowing a SageAttention-capable profile if it can be installed and validated cleanly.\n2. Keep the VibeComfy workflow source pure Python.\n   - No app-active workflow should rely on executing JSON blobs as the workflow implementation.\n   - JSON may exist only as reference/corpus/import material, not the runtime source of truth.\n3. Make environment dependencies explicit but not burdensome.\n   - Capture system packages, Python version, CUDA/Torch profile, ComfyUI/VibeComfy commits, custom node lockfile state, extra model paths, optional acceleration profile, and model asset expectations in one generated or easily maintained manifest.\n   - Avoid forcing developers to update a fragile hand-written manifest every time a workflow changes. Prefer deriving information from templates, contracts, lockfiles, and validation output.\n4. Make validation fail early.\n   - Before running expensive workflows, check the environment can import VibeComfy, restore/list custom nodes, read required model paths, validate node input schemas against the installed node classes, and resolve model assets.\n   - The onboarding/check path should catch missing required connections and missing required node inputs before RunPod execution.\n   - If a model asset is missing, the tool should report the exact expected asset and where it looked. If a URL is known, it should be able to reconcile/fetch or print a precise remediation.\n5. Make pod lifecycle safe.\n   - Never kill unrelated user pods.\n   - Tag/label/name pods launched by this tooling clearly.\n   - Provide a safe cleanup/status command that only targets pods launched for this validation flow.\n6. Integrate with current Reigh live-test tools instead of creating a parallel universe.\n   - Existing code lives primarily in `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test`, `runpod-lifecycle`, and `vibecomfy`.\n   - Use the existing live-test matrix and reports.\n   - Prebuilt should become the easy/default validation path when the volume/profile exists; fresh should remain a fallback and a way to validate bootstrap correctness.\n7. Be documented for agents and developers.\n   - Update the relevant README/skill/docs so a future agent knows when to use the prebuilt path, how to build/update it, how to validate it, and what evidence counts.\n   - Document the \"workflow onboarding\" checklist: derive/convert/fork Python workflow, validate node schemas, validate assets, run cheap env checks, run smoke, run matrix, record contract evidence.\n\nExpected implementation shape:\n- Improve or add CLIs/scripts around prebuilt env build/check/update/reconcile/status/cleanup.\n- Add tests for the new behavior.\n- Where possible, use existing abstractions in VibeComfy: template registry, model asset extraction, node lock restore, node schema inspection, workflow validation/doctor/converter tools.\n- If missing abstractions are found, add the smallest useful abstraction that removes repeated manual friction.\n- Keep changes scoped and avoid unrelated refactors.\n\nValidation requirements:\n- Local unit tests for any changed Python modules.\n- At least one remote RunPod prebuilt environment health check.\n- At least one VibeComfy workflow smoke run using the prebuilt environment.\n- Evidence should be written to the existing live-test report structure or a similarly discoverable report path.\n- Pods launched for validation must be terminated at the end unless intentionally preserved and clearly documented.\n\nKnown useful references already produced:\n- Prebuilt volume that previously passed image/edit/upscale batch: `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` (`h6ln28obus`) in `EUR-NO-1`.\n- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T013030Z/report.json` passed 8/8 image/edit/upscale cases.\n- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T012010Z/report.json` passed `z_image_turbo`.\n- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260512T204040Z/report.json` passed LTX control matrix 4/4.\n- Do not assume those are enough; this task is about making the prebuilt environment path systematic and then using it.\n\nImportant safety:\n- Do not print secrets from `.env`, process environments, or SSH `ps e` output.\n- Do not kill unrelated pods. Only clean up pods clearly launched by this flow.\n- Preserve dirty user changes unless they are directly part of this work.\n\nSuccess criteria:\n- The repo has a clear, tested prebuilt-environment flow that future agents can discover and use by default for VibeComfy validation.\n- The flow makes it obvious whether a workflow is blocked by environment setup, missing assets, invalid node inputs, or runtime workflow logic.\n- Documentation explains both the \"why\" and the commands succinctly.\n- The tooling is actually battle-tested by running at least one prebuilt RunPod health/smoke validation and recording the result.",

        Requirements:
        - Before addressing individual flags, check: does any flag suggest the plan is targeting the wrong code or the wrong root cause? If so, consider whether the plan needs a new approach rather than adjustments. Explain your reasoning.
        - Update the plan to address the significant issues.
        - Keep the plan readable and executable.
        - Return flags_addressed with the exact flag IDs you addressed.
        - Include `changes_summary` as a short plain-English summary of what changed in the revision. If there were no concrete flags, say that explicitly (for example: `No critique flags were raised; refined wording and kept the plan aligned for execution.`).
        - Preserve or improve success criteria quality. Each criterion must have a `priority` of `must`, `should`, or `info`. Promote or demote priorities if critique feedback reveals a criterion was over- or under-weighted.
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Verify that the plan remains aligned with the user's original intent, not just internal plan quality.
        - Remove unjustified scope growth. If critique raised scope creep, narrow the plan back to the original idea unless the broader work is strictly required.
        - Maintain the structural template: H1 title, ## Overview, phase sections with numbered step sections, ## Execution Order or ## Validation Order.
        - CRITICAL: Your entire revised plan markdown (all sections) must be output as the `plan` field in the structured output. The prose response must not contain the plan text.
        - CRITICAL: Return only the structured JSON object for the schema fields `plan`, `changes_summary`, `flags_addressed`, `assumptions`, `success_criteria`, and `questions`. Do not add commentary before or after the JSON object.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

  "plan": "# Implementation Plan: Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment\n\n## Overview\nThe critique does not point to a wrong root cause or a need to replace the approach. The right foundation is still the existing prebuilt flow: `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py` for contracts and probes, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py` for `rl prebuilt ...` commands, and `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py` for the actual consumer run. The flags show the plan was under-specified in places that matter: dry-run CLI behavior, separate worker/VibeComfy virtualenv profiles, app-active Python workflow enforcement, asset reconciliation, actual consumer-pod validation, deterministic volume selection, parser/dispatch/test coverage, and report metadata compatibility.\n\nThe revised plan keeps the existing architecture and tightens it. Standalone `rl prebuilt check` validates a volume through a short-lived probe pod. The live-test prebuilt path uses the same lower-level in-pod probe functions after extraction, git sync, delta sync, and model-path binding, so it validates the actual consumer pod before queueing expensive work. Asset and workflow expectations are derived from selected Reigh live-test cases and route/template metadata, not every VibeComfy ready template.\n\n## Phase 1: Contracts, Selection, And Shared Probes\n\n### Step 1: Model the real prebuilt environment (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. Add a versioned `PrebuiltHealthReport` and smaller nested records for `worker_env` and `vibecomfy_env`, each recording executable path, Python version, Torch version, CUDA version, import status, and relevant package/profile checks.\n2. Keep `PrebuiltManifest.python_version` as the worker bundle compatibility field for existing hard-fail behavior, and add explicit VibeComfy interpreter data only to the generated health report unless a bundle-format migration truly requires manifest changes.\n3. Add atomic JSON helpers for `env.health.json` next to `env.manifest.json`, preserving current SSH heredoc/write patterns.\n4. Keep the manifest as bundle compatibility truth and the health report as generated validation evidence.\n\n### Step 2: Normalize volume names and deterministic selection (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/config.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. Centralize data-center normalization for canonical names such as `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-<normalized-data-center>` without hard-coding `eu-no-1` or `eur-no-1`.\n2. Add a caller-facing `--prebuilt-data-center` option for `--variant prebuilt|auto` and pass it into `_resolve_volume()` as a filter when supplied.\n3. When multiple matching profile volumes exist and no data center/name is supplied, fail with a clear list of candidates instead of selecting the first arbitrary match.\n4. In docs, mention `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` only as an observed historical example, not as a default.\n\n### Step 3: Build reusable in-pod health probes (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)\n**Scope:** Large\n1. Refactor the current `verify_extracted_env()` checks into a shared function that accepts an already-connected SSH session, `PrebuiltEnvContract`, manifest, selected template/workflow identifiers, and an optional reconcile mode.\n2. Probe the actual remote paths for the worker venv and VibeComfy venv separately: `contract.runtime_venv_path` and `contract.runtime_vibecomfy_path/.venv`.\n3. Check mount readability, required system packages (`ffmpeg`, `zstd`, `pv`), VibeComfy import, ComfyUI import, worker Torch/CUDA, VibeComfy Torch/CUDA, SageAttention only when `attention_profile=sage`, custom-node lock restore/list sanity, schema provider availability, `extra_model_paths.yaml`, and selected model assets.\n4. Return structured issue objects grouped by layer: `environment`, `custom_nodes`, `workflow_source`, `schema`, `assets`, and `runtime_deferred`.\n\n## Phase 2: Workflow And Asset Validation\n\n### Step 4: Derive selected app-active workflows from Reigh, not all VibeComfy templates (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/matrix.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, VibeComfy loader helpers)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. Build the validation target set from the selected live-test cases, route keys, and worker route/template metadata used by `build_matrix()`, not from all `ready_template_ids()`.\n2. Include selected case names, route keys, task types, and resolved VibeComfy template ids in the health report.\n\n### Step 5: Enforce pure-Python app-active workflow sources (`vibecomfy/vibecomfy/...`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1.         Idea:
# Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment

We are migrating Reigh's app-active AI generation paths from direct Wan2GP execution to VibeComfy-backed pure-Python workflow templates. The user wants absolute feature parity with the Wan2GP path, actual RunPod validation, and tooling that prevents future agents/developers from rediscovering the same problems manually.

Current reality:
- `reigh-worker` branch: `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-control-rail-travel-matrix`
- `vibecomfy` branch: `megaplan/production-parity-templates`
- `runpod-lifecycle` branch: `megaplan/prebuilt-validation-env`
- Existing prebuilt RunPod validation volume has already proven useful but is not yet a clean, repeatable developer experience.
- Fresh installs repeatedly hit slow or flaky setup problems: network-volume stale file handles, missing system packages such as `ffmpeg`, missing custom-node assets, missing model directories in `extra_model_paths.yaml`, optional attention backends such as SageAttention, and pod lifecycle sprawl.
- A recent local fix in `reigh-worker` moved fresh/update VibeComfy virtualenvs from `/workspace/.../.venv` to `/opt/reigh-vibecomfy-live-test-venv` so package installs happen on local disk instead of the RunPod network volume. That was tested with 152 local tests and pushed.

Goal:
Build and validate a robust, reusable prebuilt environment path for Reigh VibeComfy validation. It should make the rest of the parity work easier and safer, not add a rigid second source of truth.

The target design should:
1. Provide a standard prebuilt environment for 4090-class RunPod validation.
   - Prefer RTX 4090 as the normal target because that is the user's realistic production/developer bar.
   - Keep the design able to run on larger GPUs when explicitly requested.
   - Make the environment portable when advanced kernels are unavailable, while still allowing a SageAttention-capable profile if it can be installed and validated cleanly.
2. Keep the VibeComfy workflow source pure Python.
   - No app-active workflow should rely on executing JSON blobs as the workflow implementation.
   - JSON may exist only as reference/corpus/import material, not the runtime source of truth.
3. Make environment dependencies explicit but not burdensome.
   - Capture system packages, Python version, CUDA/Torch profile, ComfyUI/VibeComfy commits, custom node lockfile state, extra model paths, optional acceleration profile, and model asset expectations in one generated or easily maintained manifest.
   - Avoid forcing developers to update a fragile hand-written manifest every time a workflow changes. Prefer deriving information from templates, contracts, lockfiles, and validation output.
4. Make validation fail early.
   - Before running expensive workflows, check the environment can import VibeComfy, restore/list custom nodes, read required model paths, validate node input schemas against the installed node classes, and resolve model assets.
   - The onboarding/check path should catch missing required connections and missing required node inputs before RunPod execution.
   - If a model asset is missing, the tool should report the exact expected asset and where it looked. If a URL is known, it should be able to reconcile/fetch or print a precise remediation.
5. Make pod lifecycle safe.
   - Never kill unrelated user pods.
   - Tag/label/name pods launched by this tooling clearly.
   - Provide a safe cleanup/status command that only targets pods launched for this validation flow.
6. Integrate with current Reigh live-test tools instead of creating a parallel universe.
   - Existing code lives primarily in `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test`, `runpod-lifecycle`, and `vibecomfy`.
   - Use the existing live-test matrix and reports.
   - Prebuilt should become the easy/default validation path when the volume/profile exists; fresh should remain a fallback and a way to validate bootstrap correctness.
7. Be documented for agents and developers.
   - Update the relevant README/skill/docs so a future agent knows when to use the prebuilt path, how to build/update it, how to validate it, and what evidence counts.
   - Document the "workflow onboarding" checklist: derive/convert/fork Python workflow, validate node schemas, validate assets, run cheap env checks, run smoke, run matrix, record contract evidence.

Expected implementation shape:
- Improve or add CLIs/scripts around prebuilt env build/check/update/reconcile/status/cleanup.
- Add tests for the new behavior.
- Where possible, use existing abstractions in VibeComfy: template registry, model asset extraction, node lock restore, node schema inspection, workflow validation/doctor/converter tools.
- If missing abstractions are found, add the smallest useful abstraction that removes repeated manual friction.
- Keep changes scoped and avoid unrelated refactors.

Validation requirements:
- Local unit tests for any changed Python modules.
- At least one remote RunPod prebuilt environment health check.
- At least one VibeComfy workflow smoke run using the prebuilt environment.
- Evidence should be written to the existing live-test report structure or a similarly discoverable report path.
- Pods launched for validation must be terminated at the end unless intentionally preserved and clearly documented.

Known useful references already produced:
- Prebuilt volume that previously passed image/edit/upscale batch: `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` (`h6ln28obus`) in `EUR-NO-1`.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T013030Z/report.json` passed 8/8 image/edit/upscale cases.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T012010Z/report.json` passed `z_image_turbo`.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260512T204040Z/report.json` passed LTX control matrix 4/4.
- Do not assume those are enough; this task is about making the prebuilt environment path systematic and then using it.

Important safety:
- Do not print secrets from `.env`, process environments, or SSH `ps e` output.
- Do not kill unrelated pods. Only clean up pods clearly launched by this flow.
- Preserve dirty user changes unless they are directly part of this work.

Success criteria:
- The repo has a clear, tested prebuilt-environment flow that future agents can discover and use by default for VibeComfy validation.
- The flow makes it obvious whether a workflow is blocked by environment setup, missing assets, invalid node inputs, or runtime workflow logic.
- Documentation explains both the "why" and the commands succinctly.
- The tooling is actually battle-tested by running at least one prebuilt RunPod health/smoke validation and recording the result.

User notes and answers:
- Resolved plan questions: (1) do not hard-code eu-no-1/eur-no-1; derive canonical prebuilt volume names from the actual RunPod data_center_id normalization and have docs mention the observed reference volume only as an example. (2) Required first remote smoke gate should be cheapest z_image_turbo, then broader image/edit/upscale or video matrix runs after base health is proven. (3) SageAttention should be an optional validated profile: implement/install/check support when requested, but portable 4090 validation must remain the default and must not be blocked by Sage installation failure unless the user explicitly requests the sage profile.

        Current plan (markdown):
        # Implementation Plan: Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment

## Overview
The critique does not point to a wrong root cause or a need to replace the approach. The right foundation is still the existing prebuilt flow: `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py` for contracts and probes, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py` for `rl prebuilt ...` commands, and `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py` for the actual consumer run. The flags show the plan was under-specified in places that matter: dry-run CLI behavior, separate worker/VibeComfy virtualenv profiles, app-active Python workflow enforcement, asset reconciliation, actual consumer-pod validation, deterministic volume selection, parser/dispatch/test coverage, and report metadata compatibility.

The revised plan keeps the existing architecture and tightens it. Standalone `rl prebuilt check` validates a volume through a short-lived probe pod. The live-test prebuilt path uses the same lower-level in-pod probe functions after extraction, git sync, delta sync, and model-path binding, so it validates the actual consumer pod before queueing expensive work. Asset and workflow expectations are derived from selected Reigh live-test cases and route/template metadata, not every VibeComfy ready template.

## Phase 1: Contracts, Selection, And Shared Probes

### Step 1: Model the real prebuilt environment (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a versioned `PrebuiltHealthReport` and smaller nested records for `worker_env` and `vibecomfy_env`, each recording executable path, Python version, Torch version, CUDA version, import status, and relevant package/profile checks.
2. Keep `PrebuiltManifest.python_version` as the worker bundle compatibility field for existing hard-fail behavior, and add explicit VibeComfy interpreter data only to the generated health report unless a bundle-format migration truly requires manifest changes.
3. Add atomic JSON helpers for `env.health.json` next to `env.manifest.json`, preserving current SSH heredoc/write patterns.
4. Keep the manifest as bundle compatibility truth and the health report as generated validation evidence.

### Step 2: Normalize volume names and deterministic selection (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/config.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Centralize data-center normalization for canonical names such as `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-<normalized-data-center>` without hard-coding `eu-no-1` or `eur-no-1`.
2. Add a caller-facing `--prebuilt-data-center` option for `--variant prebuilt|auto` and pass it into `_resolve_volume()` as a filter when supplied.
3. When multiple matching profile volumes exist and no data center/name is supplied, fail with a clear list of candidates instead of selecting the first arbitrary match.
4. In docs, mention `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` only as an observed historical example, not as a default.

### Step 3: Build reusable in-pod health probes (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Refactor the current `verify_extracted_env()` checks into a shared function that accepts an already-connected SSH session, `PrebuiltEnvContract`, manifest, selected template/workflow identifiers, and an optional reconcile mode.
2. Probe the actual remote paths for the worker venv and VibeComfy venv separately: `contract.runtime_venv_path` and `contract.runtime_vibecomfy_path/.venv`.
3. Check mount readability, required system packages (`ffmpeg`, `zstd`, `pv`), VibeComfy import, ComfyUI import, worker Torch/CUDA, VibeComfy Torch/CUDA, SageAttention only when `attention_profile=sage`, custom-node lock restore/list sanity, schema provider availability, `extra_model_paths.yaml`, and selected model assets.
4. Return structured issue objects grouped by layer: `environment`, `custom_nodes`, `workflow_source`, `schema`, `assets`, and `runtime_deferred`.

## Phase 2: Workflow And Asset Validation

### Step 4: Derive selected app-active workflows from Reigh, not all VibeComfy templates (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/matrix.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, VibeComfy loader helpers)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Build the validation target set from the selected live-test cases, route keys, and worker route/template metadata used by `build_matrix()`, not from all `ready_template_ids()`.
2. Pass that selected target set into the prebuilt health probe so unrelated audio/supplemental templates cannot block an image-only or smoke-only validation run.
3. Include selected case names, route keys, task types, and resolved VibeComfy template ids in the health report.

### Step 5: Enforce pure-Python app-active workflow sources (`vibecomfy/vibecomfy/...`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add or reuse a VibeComfy classification helper that can distinguish pure Python workflow composition from API-dict/JSON wrapper templates for a loaded template.
2. Make the prebuilt health probe fail selected app-active routes that resolve to runtime `API_WORKFLOW`/JSON-dict wrappers.
3. Keep JSON corpus/import/reference material allowed; only app-active runtime source-of-truth wrappers fail.
4. Add diagnostics that name the selected route/case/template and the file that needs conversion.

### Step 6: Add asset diagnostics and reconciliation (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`, VibeComfy model asset helpers)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Use VibeComfy model asset metadata/extraction helpers as the source of expected model assets for selected templates.
2. Report missing assets with asset name, category/subdirectory, local paths checked through `extra_model_paths.yaml`, and known URL/remediation when available.
3. Add `rl prebuilt reconcile --volume-name ... --data-center ... --case/--route-key ... --dry-run --json` that stages/fetches only known missing assets into the prebuilt volume model cache when URLs are known, using existing VibeComfy fetch/stage primitives where available.
4. Keep `check` non-mutating by default. It may print the exact `reconcile` command but should not download assets unless the user invokes reconcile.

## Phase 3: CLI Surface And Lifecycle Safety

### Step 7: Add prebuilt check/status/cleanup/reconcile parser and dispatch (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Update the prebuilt dispatch map to include `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile` in addition to `build`, `inspect`, `invalidate`, and `list`.
2. Add parser entries and help text for all new subcommands.
3. Require `--dry-run` support for `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile`; dry-run must not require `RUNPOD_API_KEY` or provision a pod.
4. Add `--json`, `--attention-profile portable|sage`, `--gpu-type`, `--container-disk-gb`, and `--keep-pod` where applicable. Portable RTX 4090 remains the default; SageAttention is opt-in and only blocks when the sage profile is explicitly selected.

### Step 8: Implement standalone `rl prebuilt check` as a probe-pod volume validator (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Provision a clearly named short-lived probe pod, attach the selected volume, read the manifest, extract or inspect bundles as needed, run the shared in-pod health probes, write `env.health.json`, print a concise result, and terminate by default.
2. Keep `--keep-pod` explicit and include pod id/reason in output when used.
3. Ensure `--dry-run` prints planned pod name, volume selection, paths, selected cases/routes/templates, and checks without credentials or network calls.

### Step 9: Add prebuilt status and cleanup safety (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/guard.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `rl prebuilt status --dry-run --json` that only lists pods matching explicit allowlisted prefixes: `reigh-livetest-builder-` and `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-`.
2. Add `rl prebuilt cleanup --older-than ... --dry-run --yes` that only terminates allowlisted prebuilt validation pods and never generic user pods.
3. Reuse existing prefix-pruning logic where practical, but keep the prebuilt allowlist visible and unit-tested.

## Phase 4: Live-Test Integration And Reports

### Step 10: Validate the actual consumer pod before queueing (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. After manifest read, hard-fail drift checks, extraction, worker git sync, VibeComfy git sync/delta install, and `_write_extra_model_paths_yaml()`, call the shared in-pod health probe directly on that same SSH session.
2. Do not shell out to `rl prebuilt check` from inside `variant_prebuilt`; the CLI validates a separate probe pod and is only for standalone volume checks.
3. Abort before `register_worker_record()`, `launch_worker`, or `queue_matrix()` when health checks find environment, pure-Python source, schema, required input/connection, or asset failures.
4. Write the health summary to the live-test run directory even on early failure when possible.

### Step 11: Add optional report metadata without breaking other variants (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/report.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, fresh/update call sites)
**Scope:** Small
1. Change `write_report(results, variant, pod_id, out_dir)` to accept an optional keyword-only metadata parameter, for example `write_report(..., metadata=None)`.
2. Have prebuilt pass volume id/name, data center, manifest path/hash, health report path/hash, selected profile, selected templates, GPU type, and check summary.
3. Leave fresh/update callers valid with no changes beyond the compatible function signature.

## Phase 5: Tests And Documentation

### Step 12: Add focused tests for contracts and CLI (`runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_cli.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_dry_run_smoke.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Test health report JSON round trip, separate worker/VibeComfy interpreter records, missing asset diagnostics, pure-Python classification failures, and schema-check issue grouping.
2. Update existing prebuilt help tests so they expect all subcommands: `build`, `inspect`, `invalidate`, `list`, `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile`.
3. Add no-credential dry-run tests for `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile`.
4. Add deterministic volume-selection tests for explicit volume name, explicit data center, multiple matching candidates, and canonical normalized names.

### Step 13: Add live-test integration tests (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_variant_prebuilt.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_dry_run_smoke.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Test that prebuilt health failure occurs before worker registration, launch, and matrix queueing.
2. Test that the live-test path calls the shared in-pod probe with the consumer pod SSH session rather than invoking the standalone CLI.
3. Test that optional report metadata preserves existing fresh/update report behavior.
4. Test that selected workflow targets come from selected live-test cases/routes, not all VibeComfy ready templates.

### Step 14: Document the operational workflow (`runpod-lifecycle/README.md`, nearest `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test` docs, relevant VibeComfy docs)
**Scope:** Small
1. Document prebuilt vs fresh, default portable RTX 4090 behavior, optional larger GPU and SageAttention profiles, and canonical volume naming derived from RunPod `data_center_id` normalization.
2. Document `build`, `check`, `reconcile`, `status`, `cleanup`, `invalidate`, and `list`, including dry-run/no-credential behavior.
3. Document workflow onboarding: derive/convert/fork pure Python workflow, validate selected route/template schemas, verify assets, run cheap prebuilt check, run `z_image_turbo` smoke, then broaden to image/edit/upscale or video matrices.
4. State that app-active runtime workflow source must be pure Python; JSON/API-dict wrappers are allowed only as reference/import/corpus material.

## Phase 6: Validation

### Step 15: Run local verification
**Scope:** Small
1. Run targeted tests first: `pytest runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_cli.py reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_variant_prebuilt.py reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_dry_run_smoke.py`.
2. Run broader affected suites after targeted tests pass.

### Step 16: Battle-test on RunPod
**Scope:** Large
1. Discover the actual current portable prebuilt volume and data center from RunPod instead of hard-coding the historical example.
2. Run `rl prebuilt check` against the selected portable 4090 profile and confirm `env.health.json` evidence is written.
3. Run the first remote smoke gate with `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py --variant prebuilt --backend vibecomfy --case z_image_turbo` using the selected volume/profile.
4. Confirm pods launched by check/smoke are terminated unless intentionally preserved with `--keep-pod`, and record any preserved pod id and reason.
5. After base health and smoke pass, run broader image/edit/upscale or video matrix validation as a follow-up gate, not as the first blocker.

## Execution Order
1. Fix contracts and shared probe modeling first, including separate worker/VibeComfy env records.
2. Add deterministic volume selection before wiring new commands that depend on it.
3. Add workflow-source and selected-template validation before asset reconciliation, so checks do not over-block unrelated templates.
4. Add parser/dispatch/dry-run behavior for new CLI verbs before remote implementations.
5. Wire the shared in-pod probe into `variant_prebuilt` only after the standalone probe and tests are stable.
6. Add report metadata compatibility and docs after command/output shape is settled.

## Validation Order
1. Start with dry-run/no-credential CLI tests.
2. Run unit tests for health report serialization, volume selection, source classification, asset diagnostics, and cleanup allowlists.
3. Run live-test integration tests proving early failure before queueing.
4. Run broader affected local tests.
5. Run real `rl prebuilt check` on RunPod.
6. Run real `z_image_turbo` prebuilt smoke and verify report evidence plus pod cleanup.

  "signals": {
    "iteration": 2,
    "idea": "# Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment\n\nWe are migrating Reigh's app-active AI generation paths from direct Wan2GP execution to VibeComfy-backed pure-Python workflow templates. The user wants absolute feature parity with the Wan2GP path, actual RunPod validation, and tooling that prevents future agents/developers from rediscovering the same problems manually.\n\nCurrent reality:\n- `reigh-worker` branch: `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-control-rail-travel-matrix`\n- `vibecomfy` branch: `megaplan/production-parity-templates`\n- `runpod-lifecycle` branch: `megaplan/prebuilt-validation-env`\n- Existing prebuilt RunPod validation volume has already proven useful but is not yet a clean, repeatable developer experience.\n- Fresh installs repeatedly hit slow or flaky setup problems: network-volume stale file handles, missing system packages such as `ffmpeg`, missing custom-node assets, missing model directories in `extra_model_paths.yaml`, optional attention backends such as SageAttention, and pod lifecycle sprawl.\n- A recent local fix in `reigh-worker` moved fresh/update VibeComfy virtualenvs from `/workspace/.../.venv` to `/opt/reigh-vibecomfy-live-test-venv` so package installs happen on local disk instead of the RunPod network volume. That was tested with 152 local tests and pushed.\n\nGoal:\nBuild and validate a robust, reusable prebuilt environment path for Reigh VibeComfy validation. It should make the rest of the parity work easier and safer, not add a rigid second source of truth.\n\nThe target design should:\n1. Provide a standard prebuilt environment for 4090-class RunPod validation.\n   - Prefer RTX 4090 as the normal target because that is the user's realistic production/developer bar.\n   - Keep the design able to run on larger GPUs when explicitly requested.\n   - Make the environment portable when advanced kernels are unavailable, while still allowing a SageAttention-capable profile if it can be installed and validated cleanly.\n2. Keep the VibeComfy workflow source pure Python.\n   - No app-active workflow should rely on executing JSON blobs as the workflow implementation.\n   - JSON may exist only as reference/corpus/import material, not the runtime source of truth.\n3. Make environment dependencies explicit but not burdensome.\n   - Capture system packages, Python version, CUDA/Torch profile, ComfyUI/VibeComfy commits, custom node lockfile state, extra model paths, optional acceleration profile, and model asset expectations in one generated or easily maintained manifest.\n   - Avoid forcing developers to update a fragile hand-written manifest every time a workflow changes. Prefer deriving information from templates, contracts, lockfiles, and validation output.\n4. Make validation fail early.\n   - Before running expensive workflows, check the environment can import VibeComfy, restore/list custom nodes, read required model paths, validate node input schemas against the installed node classes, and resolve model assets.\n   - The onboarding/check path should catch missing required connections and missing required node inputs before RunPod execution.\n   - If a model asset is missing, the tool should report the exact expected asset and where it looked. If a URL is known, it should be able to reconcile/fetch or print a precise remediation.\n5. Make pod lifecycle safe.\n   - Never kill unrelated user pods.\n   - Tag/label/name pods launched by this tooling clearly.\n   - Provide a safe cleanup/status command that only targets pods launched for this validation flow.\n6. Integrate with current Reigh live-test tools instead of creating a parallel universe.\n   - Existing code lives primarily in `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test`, `runpod-lifecycle`, and `vibecomfy`.\n   - Use the existing live-test matrix and reports.\n   - Prebuilt should become the easy/default validation path when the volume/profile exists; fresh should remain a fallback and a way to validate bootstrap correctness.\n7. Be documented for agents and developers.\n   - Update the relevant README/skill/docs so a future agent knows when to use the prebuilt path, how to build/update it, how to validate it, and what evidence counts.\n   - Document the \"workflow onboarding\" checklist: derive/convert/fork Python workflow, validate node schemas, validate assets, run cheap env checks, run smoke, run matrix, record contract evidence.\n\nExpected implementation shape:\n- Improve or add CLIs/scripts around prebuilt env build/check/update/reconcile/status/cleanup.\n- Add tests for the new behavior.\n- Where possible, use existing abstractions in VibeComfy: template registry, model asset extraction, node lock restore, node schema inspection, workflow validation/doctor/converter tools.\n- If missing abstractions are found, add the smallest useful abstraction that removes repeated manual friction.\n- Keep changes scoped and avoid unrelated refactors.\n\nValidation requirements:\n- Local unit tests for any changed Python modules.\n- At least one remote RunPod prebuilt environment health check.\n- At least one VibeComfy workflow smoke run using the prebuilt environment.\n- Evidence should be written to the existing live-test report structure or a similarly discoverable report path.\n- Pods launched for validation must be terminated at the end unless intentionally preserved and clearly documented.\n\nKnown useful references already produced:\n- Prebuilt volume that previously passed image/edit/upscale batch: `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` (`h6ln28obus`) in `EUR-NO-1`.\n- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T013030Z/report.json` passed 8/8 image/edit/upscale cases.\n- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T012010Z/report.json` passed `z_image_turbo`.\n- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260512T204040Z/report.json` passed LTX control matrix 4/4.\n- Do not assume those are enough; this task is about making the prebuilt environment path systematic and then using it.\n\nImportant safety:\n- Do not print secrets from `.env`, process environments, or SSH `ps e` output.\n- Do not kill unrelated pods. Only clean up pods clearly launched by this flow.\n- Preserve dirty user changes unless they are directly part of this work.\n\nSuccess criteria:\n- The repo has a clear, tested prebuilt-environment flow that future agents can discover and use by default for VibeComfy validation.\n- The flow makes it obvious whether a workflow is blocked by environment setup, missing assets, invalid node inputs, or runtime workflow logic.\n- Documentation explains both the \"why\" and the commands succinctly.\n- The tooling is actually battle-tested by running at least one prebuilt RunPod health/smoke validation and recording the result.",
