        Requirements:
        - Before addressing individual flags, check: does any flag suggest the plan is targeting the wrong code or the wrong root cause? If so, consider whether the plan needs a new approach rather than adjustments. Explain your reasoning.
        - Update the plan to address the significant issues.
        - Keep the plan readable and executable.
        - Return flags_addressed with the exact flag IDs you addressed.
        - Include `changes_summary` as a short plain-English summary of what changed in the revision. If there were no concrete flags, say that explicitly (for example: `No critique flags were raised; refined wording and kept the plan aligned for execution.`).
        - Preserve or improve success criteria quality. Each criterion must have a `priority` of `must`, `should`, or `info`. Promote or demote priorities if critique feedback reveals a criterion was over- or under-weighted.
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Verify that the plan remains aligned with the user's original intent, not just internal plan quality.
        - Remove unjustified scope growth. If critique raised scope creep, narrow the plan back to the original idea unless the broader work is strictly required.
        - Maintain the structural template: H1 title, ## Overview, phase sections with numbered step sections, ## Execution Order or ## Validation Order.
        - CRITICAL: Your entire revised plan markdown (all sections) must be output as the `plan` field in the structured output. The prose response must not contain the plan text.
        - CRITICAL: Return only the structured JSON object for the schema fields `plan`, `changes_summary`, `flags_addressed`, `assumptions`, `success_criteria`, and `questions`. Do not add commentary before or after the JSON object.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

> AGENT

{
  "plan": "# Implementation Plan: Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment\n\n## Overview\nThe remaining critique does not change the root approach: extend the existing prebuilt builder/consumer flow rather than creating a parallel validation system. It does tighten ownership boundaries. `reigh-worker` owns live-test case/route resolution, `vibecomfy` owns workflow/template/source and asset introspection, and `runpod-lifecycle` owns RunPod pod/volume lifecycle plus remote execution against already-resolved metadata. `runpod-lifecycle` should not learn Reigh-specific `--case` or `--route-key` semantics through local imports.\n\nThe revised plan keeps standalone `rl prebuilt check/reconcile/status/cleanup` commands, but changes their handoff surface: `reigh-worker` resolves selected cases/routes/templates into a JSON target manifest, and `runpod-lifecycle` accepts that manifest or runs remote commands inside the checked-out VibeComfy/Reigh workspace on the pod. The builder also has to enforce the worker Python version it records in `PrebuiltManifest`, rather than treating it as a declarative label.\n\n## Phase 1: Contracts, Builder Enforcement, And Selection\n\n### Step 1: Model the real prebuilt environment (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. Add a versioned `PrebuiltHealthReport` and nested records for `worker_env` and `vibecomfy_env`, each recording executable path, observed Python version, Torch version, CUDA version, import status, and profile-specific checks.\n2. Keep `PrebuiltManifest.python_version` as the worker bundle compatibility field, but require builder verification before writing it.\n3. Add atomic JSON helpers for `env.health.json` next to `env.manifest.json`, preserving current SSH heredoc/write patterns.\n4. Keep manifest compatibility evidence separate from generated health evidence.\n\n### Step 2: Enforce worker Python in the builder (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. Update `_uv_sync_builder_shell()` to accept the desired worker Python version and pass it to `uv sync` through an explicit `--python <version>` or equivalent `uv venv --python <version>` bootstrap before sync.\n2. After worker install, run the built worker venv Python and compare observed `major.minor` to `contract.python_version` before `_cmd_prebuilt_build` constructs `PrebuiltManifest`.\n3. Fail the build before bundle/archive/manifest write if the observed worker interpreter does not match the requested manifest Python version.\n4. Record the observed worker interpreter in `PrebuiltHealthReport.worker_env` and keep the manifest field trustworthy.\n\n### Step 3: Normalize volume names and deterministic selection (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/config.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. Centralize data-center normalization for canonical names such as `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-<normalized-data-center>` without hard-coding `eu-no-1` or `eur-no-1`.\n2. Add `--prebuilt-data-center` for `--variant prebuilt|auto` and pass it into `_resolve_volume()` as a filter when supplied.\n3. When multiple matching profile volumes exist and no data center/name is supplied, fail with a clear list of candidates instead of selecting the first arbitrary match.\n4. In docs, mention `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` only as an observed historical example.\n\n## Phase 2: Metadata Handoff And VibeComfy Introspection\n\n### Step 4: Add a Reigh-owned validation target manifest (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/matrix.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. Add a small resolver in `reigh-worker` that converts selected `--case`, `--task-type`, and `--route-key` arguments into a JSON target manifest containing selected case names, task types, route keys, resolved VibeComfy template ids, and expected asset groups.\n2. Write this target manifest into the live-test run directory and pass it to shared probes or `rl prebuilt check/reconcile` as `--targets-json <path>`.\n3. Keep all Reigh-specific case/route selection in `reigh-worker`; do not add `--case` or `--route-key` parsing to `runpod-lifecycle`.\n4. Ensure the target set is derived from selected live-test cases/routes, not all VibeComfy ready templates.\n\n### Step 5: Expose workflow source mode in named VibeComfy APIs (`vibecomfy/vibecomfy/registry/ready_template.py`, `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/cli_loader.py`, VibeComfy tests)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. Add a named source-mode enum or string field such as `python_composition`, `api_dict_wrapper`, or `json_reference` to the ready-template registry/loader surface that currently backs `ready_template_ids()` and `workflow_from_ready()`.\n2. Implement source classification in `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/registry/ready_template.py` or an adjacent helper used by that registry, and expose it through a function such as `ready_template_source_info(template_id)`.\n3. Use `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/cli_loader.py` only as a loader integration point; do not make callers infer source mode from compiled workflow dictionaries.\n4. Add diagnostics that name the template id and source file when a selected app-active template is still an API-dict/JSON runtime wrapper.\n\n### Step 6: Add asset introspection and resolved asset manifests (`vibecomfy` asset helpers, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. Include asset name, category/subdirectory, known URL, and expected relative locations in the Reigh-owned target manifest or a companion asset manifest.\n3. Report missing assets with asset name, category/path, local paths checked through `extra_model_paths.yaml`, and known remediation when available.\n4. Keep check non-mutating by default; it can print the exact reconcile command using the resolved manifest.\n\n## Phase 3: Shared Probes And CLI Surface\n\n### Step 7: Build reusable in-pod health probes (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)\n**Scope:** Large\n1. Refactor `verify_extracted_env()` into a shared function that accepts an already-connected SSH session, `PrebuiltEnvContract`, manifest, and optional resolved target/asset manifest data.\n2. Probe the actual remote paths separately: `contract.runtime_venv_path` for worker and `contract.runtime_vibecomfy_path/.venv` for VibeComfy.\n3. Check mount readability, required system packages (`ffmpeg`, `zstd`, `pv`), VibeComfy import, ComfyUI import, worker Torch/CUDA, VibeComfy Torch/CUDA, SageAttention only for `attention_profile=sage`, custom-node lock restore/list sanity, schema provider availability, `extra_model_paths.yaml`, selected source mode, selected node schemas, and selected model assets.\n4. Return structured issue objects grouped by `environment`, `custom_nodes`, `workflow_source`, `schema`, `assets`, and `runtime_deferred`.\n\n### Step 8: Add prebuilt check/status/cleanup/reconcile parser and dispatch (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. Add `--targets-json` or `--asset-manifest` for check/reconcile. Do not add Reigh-owned `--case` or `--route-key` flags to `runpod-lifecycle`.\n5. Add `--json`, `--attention-profile portable|sage`, `--gpu-type`, `--container-disk-gb`, and `--keep-pod` where applicable.\n\n### Step 9: Implement standalone check and reconcile with a clean boundary (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)\n**Scope:** Large\n1. `rl prebuilt check` provisions a clearly named short-lived probe pod, attaches the selected volume, reads the manifest, extracts or inspects bundles as needed, loads optional resolved target/asset metadata from `--targets-json`, runs shared in-pod probes, writes `env.health.json`, prints a concise result, and terminates by default.\n2. `rl prebuilt reconcile` accepts resolved asset metadata via `--targets-json` or `--asset-manifest`, then runs remote VibeComfy fetch/stage commands inside the pod checkout or stages known URLs directly into the model cache. It must not import Reigh live-test routing code locally.\n3. `--dry-run` for reconcile prints planned asset fetches and destinations without credentials or network calls.\n4. `--keep-pod` is explicit and includes pod id/reason in output when used.\n\n### Step 10: Add prebuilt status and cleanup safety (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/guard.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. Add `rl prebuilt cleanup --older-than ... --dry-run --yes` that only terminates allowlisted prebuilt validation pods and never generic user pods.\n3. Reuse existing prefix-pruning logic where practical, but keep the prebuilt allowlist visible and unit-tested.\n\n## Phase 4: Live-Test Integration And Reports\n\n### Step 11: Validate the actual consumer pod before queueing (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`)\n**Scope:** Medium\n1. After base health and smoke pass, run broader image/edit/upscale or video matrix validation as a follow-up gate, not as the first blocker.\n\n## Execution Order\n1.         Idea:
# Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment

We are migrating Reigh's app-active AI generation paths from direct Wan2GP execution to VibeComfy-backed pure-Python workflow templates. The user wants absolute feature parity with the Wan2GP path, actual RunPod validation, and tooling that prevents future agents/developers from rediscovering the same problems manually.

Current reality:
- `reigh-worker` branch: `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-control-rail-travel-matrix`
- `vibecomfy` branch: `megaplan/production-parity-templates`
- `runpod-lifecycle` branch: `megaplan/prebuilt-validation-env`
- Existing prebuilt RunPod validation volume has already proven useful but is not yet a clean, repeatable developer experience.
- Fresh installs repeatedly hit slow or flaky setup problems: network-volume stale file handles, missing system packages such as `ffmpeg`, missing custom-node assets, missing model directories in `extra_model_paths.yaml`, optional attention backends such as SageAttention, and pod lifecycle sprawl.
- A recent local fix in `reigh-worker` moved fresh/update VibeComfy virtualenvs from `/workspace/.../.venv` to `/opt/reigh-vibecomfy-live-test-venv` so package installs happen on local disk instead of the RunPod network volume. That was tested with 152 local tests and pushed.

Goal:
Build and validate a robust, reusable prebuilt environment path for Reigh VibeComfy validation. It should make the rest of the parity work easier and safer, not add a rigid second source of truth.

The target design should:
1. Provide a standard prebuilt environment for 4090-class RunPod validation.
   - Prefer RTX 4090 as the normal target because that is the user's realistic production/developer bar.
   - Keep the design able to run on larger GPUs when explicitly requested.
   - Make the environment portable when advanced kernels are unavailable, while still allowing a SageAttention-capable profile if it can be installed and validated cleanly.
2. Keep the VibeComfy workflow source pure Python.
   - No app-active workflow should rely on executing JSON blobs as the workflow implementation.
   - JSON may exist only as reference/corpus/import material, not the runtime source of truth.
3. Make environment dependencies explicit but not burdensome.
   - Capture system packages, Python version, CUDA/Torch profile, ComfyUI/VibeComfy commits, custom node lockfile state, extra model paths, optional acceleration profile, and model asset expectations in one generated or easily maintained manifest.
   - Avoid forcing developers to update a fragile hand-written manifest every time a workflow changes. Prefer deriving information from templates, contracts, lockfiles, and validation output.
4. Make validation fail early.
   - Before running expensive workflows, check the environment can import VibeComfy, restore/list custom nodes, read required model paths, validate node input schemas against the installed node classes, and resolve model assets.
   - The onboarding/check path should catch missing required connections and missing required node inputs before RunPod execution.
   - If a model asset is missing, the tool should report the exact expected asset and where it looked. If a URL is known, it should be able to reconcile/fetch or print a precise remediation.
5. Make pod lifecycle safe.
   - Never kill unrelated user pods.
   - Tag/label/name pods launched by this tooling clearly.
   - Provide a safe cleanup/status command that only targets pods launched for this validation flow.
6. Integrate with current Reigh live-test tools instead of creating a parallel universe.
   - Existing code lives primarily in `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test`, `runpod-lifecycle`, and `vibecomfy`.
   - Use the existing live-test matrix and reports.
   - Prebuilt should become the easy/default validation path when the volume/profile exists; fresh should remain a fallback and a way to validate bootstrap correctness.
7. Be documented for agents and developers.
   - Update the relevant README/skill/docs so a future agent knows when to use the prebuilt path, how to build/update it, how to validate it, and what evidence counts.
   - Document the "workflow onboarding" checklist: derive/convert/fork Python workflow, validate node schemas, validate assets, run cheap env checks, run smoke, run matrix, record contract evidence.

Expected implementation shape:
- Improve or add CLIs/scripts around prebuilt env build/check/update/reconcile/status/cleanup.
- Add tests for the new behavior.
- Where possible, use existing abstractions in VibeComfy: template registry, model asset extraction, node lock restore, node schema inspection, workflow validation/doctor/converter tools.
- If missing abstractions are found, add the smallest useful abstraction that removes repeated manual friction.
- Keep changes scoped and avoid unrelated refactors.

Validation requirements:
- Local unit tests for any changed Python modules.
- At least one remote RunPod prebuilt environment health check.
- At least one VibeComfy workflow smoke run using the prebuilt environment.
- Evidence should be written to the existing live-test report structure or a similarly discoverable report path.
- Pods launched for validation must be terminated at the end unless intentionally preserved and clearly documented.

Known useful references already produced:
- Prebuilt volume that previously passed image/edit/upscale batch: `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` (`h6ln28obus`) in `EUR-NO-1`.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T013030Z/report.json` passed 8/8 image/edit/upscale cases.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T012010Z/report.json` passed `z_image_turbo`.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260512T204040Z/report.json` passed LTX control matrix 4/4.
- Do not assume those are enough; this task is about making the prebuilt environment path systematic and then using it.

Important safety:
- Do not print secrets from `.env`, process environments, or SSH `ps e` output.
- Do not kill unrelated pods. Only clean up pods clearly launched by this flow.
- Preserve dirty user changes unless they are directly part of this work.

Success criteria:
- The repo has a clear, tested prebuilt-environment flow that future agents can discover and use by default for VibeComfy validation.
- The flow makes it obvious whether a workflow is blocked by environment setup, missing assets, invalid node inputs, or runtime workflow logic.
- Documentation explains both the "why" and the commands succinctly.
- The tooling is actually battle-tested by running at least one prebuilt RunPod health/smoke validation and recording the result.

User notes and answers:
- Resolved plan questions: (1) do not hard-code eu-no-1/eur-no-1; derive canonical prebuilt volume names from the actual RunPod data_center_id normalization and have docs mention the observed reference volume only as an example. (2) Required first remote smoke gate should be cheapest z_image_turbo, then broader image/edit/upscale or video matrix runs after base health is proven. (3) SageAttention should be an optional validated profile: implement/install/check support when requested, but portable 4090 validation must remain the default and must not be blocked by Sage installation failure unless the user explicitly requests the sage profile.

        Current plan (markdown):
        # Implementation Plan: Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment

## Overview
The remaining critique does not change the root approach: extend the existing prebuilt builder/consumer flow rather than creating a parallel validation system. It does tighten ownership boundaries. `reigh-worker` owns live-test case/route resolution, `vibecomfy` owns workflow/template/source and asset introspection, and `runpod-lifecycle` owns RunPod pod/volume lifecycle plus remote execution against already-resolved metadata. `runpod-lifecycle` should not learn Reigh-specific `--case` or `--route-key` semantics through local imports.

The revised plan keeps standalone `rl prebuilt check/reconcile/status/cleanup` commands, but changes their handoff surface: `reigh-worker` resolves selected cases/routes/templates into a JSON target manifest, and `runpod-lifecycle` accepts that manifest or runs remote commands inside the checked-out VibeComfy/Reigh workspace on the pod. The builder also has to enforce the worker Python version it records in `PrebuiltManifest`, rather than treating it as a declarative label.

## Phase 1: Contracts, Builder Enforcement, And Selection

### Step 1: Model the real prebuilt environment (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a versioned `PrebuiltHealthReport` and nested records for `worker_env` and `vibecomfy_env`, each recording executable path, observed Python version, Torch version, CUDA version, import status, and profile-specific checks.
2. Keep `PrebuiltManifest.python_version` as the worker bundle compatibility field, but require builder verification before writing it.
3. Add atomic JSON helpers for `env.health.json` next to `env.manifest.json`, preserving current SSH heredoc/write patterns.
4. Keep manifest compatibility evidence separate from generated health evidence.

### Step 2: Enforce worker Python in the builder (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Update `_uv_sync_builder_shell()` to accept the desired worker Python version and pass it to `uv sync` through an explicit `--python <version>` or equivalent `uv venv --python <version>` bootstrap before sync.
2. After worker install, run the built worker venv Python and compare observed `major.minor` to `contract.python_version` before `_cmd_prebuilt_build` constructs `PrebuiltManifest`.
3. Fail the build before bundle/archive/manifest write if the observed worker interpreter does not match the requested manifest Python version.
4. Record the observed worker interpreter in `PrebuiltHealthReport.worker_env` and keep the manifest field trustworthy.

### Step 3: Normalize volume names and deterministic selection (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/config.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Centralize data-center normalization for canonical names such as `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-<normalized-data-center>` without hard-coding `eu-no-1` or `eur-no-1`.
2. Add `--prebuilt-data-center` for `--variant prebuilt|auto` and pass it into `_resolve_volume()` as a filter when supplied.
3. When multiple matching profile volumes exist and no data center/name is supplied, fail with a clear list of candidates instead of selecting the first arbitrary match.
4. In docs, mention `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` only as an observed historical example.

## Phase 2: Metadata Handoff And VibeComfy Introspection

### Step 4: Add a Reigh-owned validation target manifest (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/matrix.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a small resolver in `reigh-worker` that converts selected `--case`, `--task-type`, and `--route-key` arguments into a JSON target manifest containing selected case names, task types, route keys, resolved VibeComfy template ids, and expected asset groups.
2. Write this target manifest into the live-test run directory and pass it to shared probes or `rl prebuilt check/reconcile` as `--targets-json <path>`.
3. Keep all Reigh-specific case/route selection in `reigh-worker`; do not add `--case` or `--route-key` parsing to `runpod-lifecycle`.
4. Ensure the target set is derived from selected live-test cases/routes, not all VibeComfy ready templates.

### Step 5: Expose workflow source mode in named VibeComfy APIs (`vibecomfy/vibecomfy/registry/ready_template.py`, `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/cli_loader.py`, VibeComfy tests)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a named source-mode enum or string field such as `python_composition`, `api_dict_wrapper`, or `json_reference` to the ready-template registry/loader surface that currently backs `ready_template_ids()` and `workflow_from_ready()`.
2. Implement source classification in `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/registry/ready_template.py` or an adjacent helper used by that registry, and expose it through a function such as `ready_template_source_info(template_id)`.
3. Use `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/cli_loader.py` only as a loader integration point; do not make callers infer source mode from compiled workflow dictionaries.
4. Add diagnostics that name the template id and source file when a selected app-active template is still an API-dict/JSON runtime wrapper.

### Step 6: Add asset introspection and resolved asset manifests (`vibecomfy` asset helpers, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Use VibeComfy model asset metadata/extraction helpers as the source of expected model assets for selected templates.
2. Include asset name, category/subdirectory, known URL, and expected relative locations in the Reigh-owned target manifest or a companion asset manifest.
3. Report missing assets with asset name, category/path, local paths checked through `extra_model_paths.yaml`, and known remediation when available.
4. Keep check non-mutating by default; it can print the exact reconcile command using the resolved manifest.

## Phase 3: Shared Probes And CLI Surface

### Step 7: Build reusable in-pod health probes (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Refactor `verify_extracted_env()` into a shared function that accepts an already-connected SSH session, `PrebuiltEnvContract`, manifest, and optional resolved target/asset manifest data.
2. Probe the actual remote paths separately: `contract.runtime_venv_path` for worker and `contract.runtime_vibecomfy_path/.venv` for VibeComfy.
3. Check mount readability, required system packages (`ffmpeg`, `zstd`, `pv`), VibeComfy import, ComfyUI import, worker Torch/CUDA, VibeComfy Torch/CUDA, SageAttention only for `attention_profile=sage`, custom-node lock restore/list sanity, schema provider availability, `extra_model_paths.yaml`, selected source mode, selected node schemas, and selected model assets.
4. Return structured issue objects grouped by `environment`, `custom_nodes`, `workflow_source`, `schema`, `assets`, and `runtime_deferred`.

### Step 8: Add prebuilt check/status/cleanup/reconcile parser and dispatch (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Update the prebuilt dispatch map to include `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile` in addition to `build`, `inspect`, `invalidate`, and `list`.
2. Add parser entries and help text for all new subcommands.
3. Require `--dry-run` support for `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile`; dry-run must not require `RUNPOD_API_KEY` or provision a pod.
4. Add `--targets-json` or `--asset-manifest` for check/reconcile. Do not add Reigh-owned `--case` or `--route-key` flags to `runpod-lifecycle`.
5. Add `--json`, `--attention-profile portable|sage`, `--gpu-type`, `--container-disk-gb`, and `--keep-pod` where applicable.

### Step 9: Implement standalone check and reconcile with a clean boundary (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. `rl prebuilt check` provisions a clearly named short-lived probe pod, attaches the selected volume, reads the manifest, extracts or inspects bundles as needed, loads optional resolved target/asset metadata from `--targets-json`, runs shared in-pod probes, writes `env.health.json`, prints a concise result, and terminates by default.
2. `rl prebuilt reconcile` accepts resolved asset metadata via `--targets-json` or `--asset-manifest`, then runs remote VibeComfy fetch/stage commands inside the pod checkout or stages known URLs directly into the model cache. It must not import Reigh live-test routing code locally.
3. `--dry-run` for reconcile prints planned asset fetches and destinations without credentials or network calls.
4. `--keep-pod` is explicit and includes pod id/reason in output when used.

### Step 10: Add prebuilt status and cleanup safety (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/guard.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `rl prebuilt status --dry-run --json` that only lists pods matching explicit allowlisted prefixes: `reigh-livetest-builder-` and `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-`.
2. Add `rl prebuilt cleanup --older-than ... --dry-run --yes` that only terminates allowlisted prebuilt validation pods and never generic user pods.
3. Reuse existing prefix-pruning logic where practical, but keep the prebuilt allowlist visible and unit-tested.

## Phase 4: Live-Test Integration And Reports

### Step 11: Validate the actual consumer pod before queueing (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. After manifest read, hard-fail drift checks, extraction, worker git sync, VibeComfy git sync/delta install, and `_write_extra_model_paths_yaml()`, call the shared in-pod health probe directly on that same SSH session.
2. Pass the Reigh-owned target manifest to the probe so selected app-active routes are validated without `runpod-lifecycle` resolving cases/routes.
3. Do not shell out to `rl prebuilt check` from inside `variant_prebuilt`; the CLI validates a separate probe pod and is only for standalone volume checks.
4. Abort before `register_worker_record()`, `launch_worker`, or `queue_matrix()` when health checks find environment, pure-Python source, schema, required input/connection, or asset failures.
5. Write the health summary and target manifest to the live-test run directory even on early failure when possible.

### Step 12: Add optional report metadata without breaking other variants (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/report.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, fresh/update call sites)
**Scope:** Small
1. Change `write_report(results, variant, pod_id, out_dir)` to accept an optional keyword-only metadata parameter, for example `write_report(..., metadata=None)`.
2. Have prebuilt pass volume id/name, data center, manifest path/hash, health report path/hash, target manifest path/hash, selected profile, selected templates, GPU type, and check summary.
3. Leave fresh/update callers valid with no changes beyond the compatible function signature.

## Phase 5: Tests And Documentation

### Step 13: Add focused tests for builder, contracts, and CLI (`runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_cli.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_dry_run_smoke.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Test that the builder sync command pins the requested worker Python version and fails before manifest write on observed mismatch.
2. Test health report JSON round trip, separate worker/VibeComfy interpreter records, missing asset diagnostics, and schema-check issue grouping.
3. Update existing prebuilt help tests so they expect all subcommands: `build`, `inspect`, `invalidate`, `list`, `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile`.
4. Add no-credential dry-run tests for `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile`.
5. Add deterministic volume-selection tests for explicit volume name, explicit data center, multiple matching candidates, and canonical normalized names.

### Step 14: Add VibeComfy and live-test integration tests (`vibecomfy/tests/...`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_variant_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Test `ready_template_source_info()` or equivalent on pure Python templates, API-dict wrapper templates, and JSON reference/corpus paths.
2. Test that selected app-active API-dict wrappers fail validation with template id and source file in the diagnostic.
3. Test that Reigh target manifest generation resolves selected live-test cases/routes and excludes unrelated ready templates.
4. Test that prebuilt health failure occurs before worker registration, launch, and matrix queueing.
5. Test that optional report metadata preserves existing fresh/update report behavior.
6. Test that `runpod-lifecycle reconcile` consumes resolved metadata and does not parse Reigh `--case` or `--route-key` flags.

### Step 15: Document the operational workflow (`runpod-lifecycle/README.md`, nearest `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test` docs, relevant VibeComfy docs)
**Scope:** Small
1. Document prebuilt vs fresh, default portable RTX 4090 behavior, optional larger GPU and SageAttention profiles, and canonical volume naming from RunPod `data_center_id` normalization.
2. Document `build`, `check`, `reconcile`, `status`, `cleanup`, `invalidate`, and `list`, including dry-run/no-credential behavior and the `--targets-json` handoff.
3. Document workflow onboarding: derive/convert/fork pure Python workflow, validate selected route/template schemas, verify assets, run cheap prebuilt check, run `z_image_turbo` smoke, then broaden to image/edit/upscale or video matrices.
4. State that app-active runtime workflow source must be pure Python; JSON/API-dict wrappers are allowed only as reference/import/corpus material.

## Phase 6: Validation

### Step 16: Run local verification
**Scope:** Small
1. Run targeted tests first: `pytest runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_cli.py reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_variant_prebuilt.py reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_dry_run_smoke.py` plus the VibeComfy source-classification tests.
2. Run broader affected suites after targeted tests pass.

### Step 17: Battle-test on RunPod
**Scope:** Large
1. Discover the actual current portable prebuilt volume and data center from RunPod instead of hard-coding the historical example.
2. Run `rl prebuilt check --targets-json <resolved-targets.json>` against the selected portable 4090 profile and confirm `env.health.json` evidence is written.
3. Run the first remote smoke gate with `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py --variant prebuilt --backend vibecomfy --case z_image_turbo` using the selected volume/profile.
4. Confirm pods launched by check/smoke are terminated unless intentionally preserved with `--keep-pod`, and record any preserved pod id and reason.
5. After base health and smoke pass, run broader image/edit/upscale or video matrix validation as a follow-up gate, not as the first blocker.

## Execution Order
1. Enforce builder worker Python version before trusting or expanding manifest/health evidence.
2. Add deterministic volume selection before wiring new commands that depend on volume/profile names.
3. Add VibeComfy source-mode API and Reigh target-manifest generation before asset reconciliation.
4. Add parser/dispatch/dry-run behavior for new CLI verbs with `--targets-json` handoff before remote implementations.
5. Wire the shared in-pod probe into `variant_prebuilt` after target metadata and probes are stable.
6. Add report metadata compatibility and docs after command/output shape is settled.

## Validation Order
1. Start with builder Python enforcement and dry-run/no-credential CLI tests.
2. Run unit tests for health report serialization, volume selection, source classification, target-manifest handoff, asset diagnostics, and cleanup allowlists.
3. Run live-test integration tests proving early failure before queueing.
4. Run broader affected local tests.
5. Run real `rl prebuilt check --targets-json ...` on RunPod.
6. Run real `z_image_turbo` prebuilt smoke and verify report evidence plus pod cleanup.

  "robustness": "standard",
  "signals": {
    "iteration": 3,
    "idea": "# Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment\n\nWe are migrating Reigh's app-active AI generation paths from direct Wan2GP execution to VibeComfy-backed pure-Python workflow templates. The user wants absolute feature parity with the Wan2GP path, actual RunPod validation, and tooling that prevents future agents/developers from rediscovering the same problems manually.\n\nCurrent reality:\n- `reigh-worker` branch: `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-control-rail-travel-matrix`\n- `vibecomfy` branch: `megaplan/production-parity-templates`\n- `runpod-lifecycle` branch: `megaplan/prebuilt-validation-env`\n- Existing prebuilt RunPod validation volume has already proven useful but is not yet a clean, repeatable developer experience.\n- Fresh installs repeatedly hit slow or flaky setup problems: network-volume stale file handles, missing system packages such as `ffmpeg`, missing custom-node assets, missing model directories in `extra_model_paths.yaml`, optional attention backends such as SageAttention, and pod lifecycle sprawl.\n- A recent local fix in `reigh-worker` moved fresh/update VibeComfy virtualenvs from `/workspace/.../.venv` to `/opt/reigh-vibecomfy-live-test-venv` so package installs happen on local disk instead of the RunPod network volume. That was tested with 152 local tests and pushed.\n\nGoal:\nBuild and validate a robust, reusable prebuilt environment path for Reigh VibeComfy validation. It should make the rest of the parity work easier and safer, not add a rigid second source of truth.\n\nThe target design should:\n1. Provide a standard prebuilt environment for 4090-class RunPod validation.\n   - Prefer RTX 4090 as the normal target because that is the user's realistic production/developer bar.\n   - Keep the design able to run on larger GPUs when explicitly requested.\n   - Make the environment portable when advanced kernels are unavailable, while still allowing a SageAttention-capable profile if it can be installed and validated cleanly.\n2. Keep the VibeComfy workflow source pure Python.\n   - No app-active workflow should rely on executing JSON blobs as the workflow implementation.\n   - JSON may exist only as reference/corpus/import material, not the runtime source of truth.\n3. Make environment dependencies explicit but not burdensome.\n   - Capture system packages, Python version, CUDA/Torch profile, ComfyUI/VibeComfy commits, custom node lockfile state, extra model paths, optional acceleration profile, and model asset expectations in one generated or easily maintained manifest.\n   - Avoid forcing developers to update a fragile hand-written manifest every time a workflow changes. Prefer deriving information from templates, contracts, lockfiles, and validation output.\n4. Make validation fail early.\n   - Before running expensive workflows, check the environment can import VibeComfy, restore/list custom nodes, read required model paths, validate node input schemas against the installed node classes, and resolve model assets.\n   - The onboarding/check path should catch missing required connections and missing required node inputs before RunPod execution.\n   - If a model asset is missing, the tool should report the exact expected asset and where it looked. If a URL is known, it should be able to reconcile/fetch or print a precise remediation.\n5. Make pod lifecycle safe.\n   - Never kill unrelated user pods.\n   - Tag/label/name pods launched by this tooling clearly.\n   - Provide a safe cleanup/status command that only targets pods launched for this validation flow.\n6. Integrate with current Reigh live-test tools instead of creating a parallel universe.\n   - Existing code lives primarily in `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test`, `runpod-lifecycle`, and `vibecomfy`.\n   - Use the existing live-test matrix and reports.\n   - Prebuilt should become the easy/default validation path when the volume/profile exists; fresh should remain a fallback and a way to validate bootstrap correctness.\n7. Be documented for agents and developers.\n   - Update the relevant README/skill/docs so a future agent knows when to use the prebuilt path, how to build/update it, how to validate it, and what evidence counts.\n   - Document the \"workflow onboarding\" checklist: derive/convert/fork Python workflow, validate node schemas, validate assets, run cheap env checks, run smoke, run matrix, record contract evidence.\n\nExpected implementation shape:\n- Improve or add CLIs/scripts around prebuilt env build/check/update/reconcile/status/cleanup.\n- Add tests for the new behavior.\n- Where possible, use existing abstractions in VibeComfy: template registry, model asset extraction, node lock restore, node schema inspection, workflow validation/doctor/converter tools.\n- If missing abstractions are found, add the smallest useful abstraction that removes repeated manual friction.\n- Keep changes scoped and avoid unrelated refactors.\n\nValidation requirements:\n- Local unit tests for any changed Python modules.\n- At least one remote RunPod prebuilt environment health check.\n- At least one VibeComfy workflow smoke run using the prebuilt environment.\n- Evidence should be written to the existing live-test report structure or a similarly discoverable report path.\n- Pods launched for validation must be terminated at the end unless intentionally preserved and clearly documented.\n\nKnown useful references already produced:\n- Prebuilt volume that previously passed image/edit/upscale batch: `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` (`h6ln28obus`) in `EUR-NO-1`.\n- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T013030Z/report.json` passed 8/8 image/edit/upscale cases.\n- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T012010Z/report.json` passed `z_image_turbo`.\n- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260512T204040Z/report.json` passed LTX control matrix 4/4.\n- Do not assume those are enough; this task is about making the prebuilt environment path systematic and then using it.\n\nImportant safety:\n- Do not print secrets from `.env`, process environments, or SSH `ps e` output.\n- Do not kill unrelated pods. Only clean up pods clearly launched by this flow.\n- Preserve dirty user changes unless they are directly part of this work.\n\nSuccess criteria:\n- The repo has a clear, tested prebuilt-environment flow that future agents can discover and use by default for VibeComfy validation.\n- The flow makes it obvious whether a workflow is blocked by environment setup, missing assets, invalid node inputs, or runtime workflow logic.\n- Documentation explains both the \"why\" and the commands succinctly.\n- The tooling is actually battle-tested by running at least one prebuilt RunPod health/smoke validation and recording the result.",

        Requirements:
        - Before addressing individual flags, check: does any flag suggest the plan is targeting the wrong code or the wrong root cause? If so, consider whether the plan needs a new approach rather than adjustments. Explain your reasoning.
        - Update the plan to address the significant issues.
        - Keep the plan readable and executable.
        - Return flags_addressed with the exact flag IDs you addressed.
        - Include `changes_summary` as a short plain-English summary of what changed in the revision. If there were no concrete flags, say that explicitly (for example: `No critique flags were raised; refined wording and kept the plan aligned for execution.`).
        - Preserve or improve success criteria quality. Each criterion must have a `priority` of `must`, `should`, or `info`. Promote or demote priorities if critique feedback reveals a criterion was over- or under-weighted.
        - Each success criterion should include a `requires` field listing the capabilities needed for verification. Valid capability strings: `run_shell`, `read_files`, `run_tests`, `parse_diff`, `read_build_output`, `run_linter` (container), `drive_browser`, `inspect_runtime_ui`, `observe_runtime_logs`, `subjective_judgment`, `verify_physical_device` (human). `must` criteria MUST have non-empty `requires`. Example: `{"criterion": "All tests pass", "priority": "must", "requires": ["run_tests"]}`.
        - Verify that the plan remains aligned with the user's original intent, not just internal plan quality.
        - Remove unjustified scope growth. If critique raised scope creep, narrow the plan back to the original idea unless the broader work is strictly required.
        - Maintain the structural template: H1 title, ## Overview, phase sections with numbered step sections, ## Execution Order or ## Validation Order.
        - CRITICAL: Your entire revised plan markdown (all sections) must be output as the `plan` field in the structured output. The prose response must not contain the plan text.
        - CRITICAL: Return only the structured JSON object for the schema fields `plan`, `changes_summary`, `flags_addressed`, `assumptions`, `success_criteria`, and `questions`. Do not add commentary before or after the JSON object.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

        Idea:
# Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment

We are migrating Reigh's app-active AI generation paths from direct Wan2GP execution to VibeComfy-backed pure-Python workflow templates. The user wants absolute feature parity with the Wan2GP path, actual RunPod validation, and tooling that prevents future agents/developers from rediscovering the same problems manually.

Current reality:
- `reigh-worker` branch: `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-control-rail-travel-matrix`
- `vibecomfy` branch: `megaplan/production-parity-templates`
- `runpod-lifecycle` branch: `megaplan/prebuilt-validation-env`
- Existing prebuilt RunPod validation volume has already proven useful but is not yet a clean, repeatable developer experience.
- Fresh installs repeatedly hit slow or flaky setup problems: network-volume stale file handles, missing system packages such as `ffmpeg`, missing custom-node assets, missing model directories in `extra_model_paths.yaml`, optional attention backends such as SageAttention, and pod lifecycle sprawl.
- A recent local fix in `reigh-worker` moved fresh/update VibeComfy virtualenvs from `/workspace/.../.venv` to `/opt/reigh-vibecomfy-live-test-venv` so package installs happen on local disk instead of the RunPod network volume. That was tested with 152 local tests and pushed.

Goal:
Build and validate a robust, reusable prebuilt environment path for Reigh VibeComfy validation. It should make the rest of the parity work easier and safer, not add a rigid second source of truth.

The target design should:
1. Provide a standard prebuilt environment for 4090-class RunPod validation.
   - Prefer RTX 4090 as the normal target because that is the user's realistic production/developer bar.
   - Keep the design able to run on larger GPUs when explicitly requested.
   - Make the environment portable when advanced kernels are unavailable, while still allowing a SageAttention-capable profile if it can be installed and validated cleanly.
2. Keep the VibeComfy workflow source pure Python.
   - No app-active workflow should rely on executing JSON blobs as the workflow implementation.
   - JSON may exist only as reference/corpus/import material, not the runtime source of truth.
3. Make environment dependencies explicit but not burdensome.
   - Capture system packages, Python version, CUDA/Torch profile, ComfyUI/VibeComfy commits, custom node lockfile state, extra model paths, optional acceleration profile, and model asset expectations in one generated or easily maintained manifest.
   - Avoid forcing developers to update a fragile hand-written manifest every time a workflow changes. Prefer deriving information from templates, contracts, lockfiles, and validation output.
4. Make validation fail early.
   - Before running expensive workflows, check the environment can import VibeComfy, restore/list custom nodes, read required model paths, validate node input schemas against the installed node classes, and resolve model assets.
   - The onboarding/check path should catch missing required connections and missing required node inputs before RunPod execution.
   - If a model asset is missing, the tool should report the exact expected asset and where it looked. If a URL is known, it should be able to reconcile/fetch or print a precise remediation.
5. Make pod lifecycle safe.
   - Never kill unrelated user pods.
   - Tag/label/name pods launched by this tooling clearly.
   - Provide a safe cleanup/status command that only targets pods launched for this validation flow.
6. Integrate with current Reigh live-test tools instead of creating a parallel universe.
   - Existing code lives primarily in `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test`, `runpod-lifecycle`, and `vibecomfy`.
   - Use the existing live-test matrix and reports.
   - Prebuilt should become the easy/default validation path when the volume/profile exists; fresh should remain a fallback and a way to validate bootstrap correctness.
7. Be documented for agents and developers.
   - Update the relevant README/skill/docs so a future agent knows when to use the prebuilt path, how to build/update it, how to validate it, and what evidence counts.
   - Document the "workflow onboarding" checklist: derive/convert/fork Python workflow, validate node schemas, validate assets, run cheap env checks, run smoke, run matrix, record contract evidence.

Expected implementation shape:
- Improve or add CLIs/scripts around prebuilt env build/check/update/reconcile/status/cleanup.
- Add tests for the new behavior.
- Where possible, use existing abstractions in VibeComfy: template registry, model asset extraction, node lock restore, node schema inspection, workflow validation/doctor/converter tools.
- If missing abstractions are found, add the smallest useful abstraction that removes repeated manual friction.
- Keep changes scoped and avoid unrelated refactors.

Validation requirements:
- Local unit tests for any changed Python modules.
- At least one remote RunPod prebuilt environment health check.
- At least one VibeComfy workflow smoke run using the prebuilt environment.
- Evidence should be written to the existing live-test report structure or a similarly discoverable report path.
- Pods launched for validation must be terminated at the end unless intentionally preserved and clearly documented.

Known useful references already produced:
- Prebuilt volume that previously passed image/edit/upscale batch: `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` (`h6ln28obus`) in `EUR-NO-1`.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T013030Z/report.json` passed 8/8 image/edit/upscale cases.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T012010Z/report.json` passed `z_image_turbo`.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260512T204040Z/report.json` passed LTX control matrix 4/4.
- Do not assume those are enough; this task is about making the prebuilt environment path systematic and then using it.

Important safety:
- Do not print secrets from `.env`, process environments, or SSH `ps e` output.
- Do not kill unrelated pods. Only clean up pods clearly launched by this flow.
- Preserve dirty user changes unless they are directly part of this work.

Success criteria:
- The repo has a clear, tested prebuilt-environment flow that future agents can discover and use by default for VibeComfy validation.
- The flow makes it obvious whether a workflow is blocked by environment setup, missing assets, invalid node inputs, or runtime workflow logic.
- Documentation explains both the "why" and the commands succinctly.
- The tooling is actually battle-tested by running at least one prebuilt RunPod health/smoke validation and recording the result.

User notes and answers:
- Resolved plan questions: (1) do not hard-code eu-no-1/eur-no-1; derive canonical prebuilt volume names from the actual RunPod data_center_id normalization and have docs mention the observed reference volume only as an example. (2) Required first remote smoke gate should be cheapest z_image_turbo, then broader image/edit/upscale or video matrix runs after base health is proven. (3) SageAttention should be an optional validated profile: implement/install/check support when requested, but portable 4090 validation must remain the default and must not be blocked by Sage installation failure unless the user explicitly requests the sage profile.

        Current plan (markdown):
        # Implementation Plan: Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment

## Overview
The remaining critique still does not change the root approach: extend the existing prebuilt builder/consumer flow rather than creating a parallel validation system. It does require one more boundary correction. `reigh-worker` owns live-test case/route selection, but it must not import `vibecomfy` locally because the packages have different dependency and Python-version contracts. `vibecomfy` owns template/source/asset introspection and should emit JSON through its own CLI or through commands run inside the checked-out VibeComfy environment on the pod. `runpod-lifecycle` owns RunPod pod/volume lifecycle and consumes resolved JSON metadata.

The final ownership model is: `reigh-worker` writes a small target manifest with selected case/route/template ids only; VibeComfy enriches that manifest with source-mode, schema, and model-asset metadata using its own Python environment; `runpod-lifecycle` and the live-test prebuilt consumer validate or reconcile from those JSON artifacts without importing across package boundaries.

## Phase 1: Contracts, Builder Enforcement, And Selection

### Step 1: Model the real prebuilt environment (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a versioned `PrebuiltHealthReport` and nested records for `worker_env` and `vibecomfy_env`, each recording executable path, observed Python version, Torch version, CUDA version, import status, and profile-specific checks.
2. Keep `PrebuiltManifest.python_version` as the worker bundle compatibility field, but require builder verification before writing it.
3. Add atomic JSON helpers for `env.health.json` next to `env.manifest.json`, preserving current SSH heredoc/write patterns.
4. Keep manifest compatibility evidence separate from generated health evidence.

### Step 2: Enforce worker Python in the builder (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Update `_uv_sync_builder_shell()` to accept the desired worker Python version and pass it to `uv sync` through an explicit `--python <version>` or equivalent `uv venv --python <version>` bootstrap before sync.
2. After worker install, run the built worker venv Python and compare observed `major.minor` to `contract.python_version` before `_cmd_prebuilt_build` constructs `PrebuiltManifest`.
3. Fail the build before bundle/archive/manifest write if the observed worker interpreter does not match the requested manifest Python version.
4. Record the observed worker interpreter in `PrebuiltHealthReport.worker_env` and keep the manifest field trustworthy.

### Step 3: Normalize volume names and deterministic selection (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/config.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Centralize data-center normalization for canonical names such as `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-<normalized-data-center>` without hard-coding `eu-no-1` or `eur-no-1`.
2. Add `--prebuilt-data-center` for `--variant prebuilt|auto` and pass it into `_resolve_volume()` as a filter when supplied.
3. When multiple matching profile volumes exist and no data center/name is supplied, fail with a clear list of candidates instead of selecting the first arbitrary match.
4. In docs, mention `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` only as an observed historical example.

## Phase 2: Metadata Handoff And VibeComfy Introspection

### Step 4: Add a Reigh-owned target manifest without VibeComfy imports (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/matrix.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a resolver in `reigh-worker` that converts selected `--case`, `--task-type`, and `--route-key` arguments into a JSON target manifest containing selected case names, task types, route keys, and resolved VibeComfy template ids.
2. Do not include VibeComfy-derived asset metadata in this Reigh step, and do not import `vibecomfy` from `reigh-worker`.
3. Write the target manifest into the live-test run directory and pass it to the consumer pod probe or standalone CLI as `--targets-json <path>`.
4. Ensure the target set is derived from selected live-test cases/routes, not all VibeComfy ready templates.

### Step 5: Expose workflow source mode in named VibeComfy APIs (`vibecomfy/vibecomfy/registry/ready_template.py`, `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/cli_loader.py`, VibeComfy tests)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a named source-mode enum or string field such as `python_composition`, `api_dict_wrapper`, or `json_reference` to the ready-template registry/loader surface that currently backs `ready_template_ids()` and `workflow_from_ready()`.
2. Implement source classification in `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/registry/ready_template.py` or an adjacent helper used by that registry, and expose it through a function such as `ready_template_source_info(template_id)`.
3. Use `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/cli_loader.py` only as a loader integration point; do not make callers infer source mode from compiled workflow dictionaries.
4. Add diagnostics that name the template id and source file when a selected app-active template is still an API-dict/JSON runtime wrapper.

### Step 6: Add VibeComfy-owned target enrichment CLI (`vibecomfy/vibecomfy/commands/...`, VibeComfy asset helpers)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a VibeComfy CLI command or subcommand that accepts Reigh’s target manifest JSON and emits an enriched validation manifest containing template source mode, schema-check inputs, expected model assets, asset categories/subdirectories, known URLs, and expected relative locations.
2. Run this command with VibeComfy’s own Python environment: locally only when a VibeComfy checkout/python is explicitly supplied, or remotely inside `contract.runtime_vibecomfy_path/.venv/bin/python` after the prebuilt VibeComfy checkout has been extracted/synced.
3. Keep `reigh-worker` as a JSON producer/consumer only. It may invoke the VibeComfy CLI as a subprocess when an explicit VibeComfy Python/check-out path is available, but it must not import VibeComfy modules.
4. Keep `runpod-lifecycle` as a JSON consumer/remote-command runner only. It must not import Reigh or VibeComfy package APIs locally.

### Step 7: Add asset diagnostics and resolved asset manifests (`vibecomfy` enrichment output, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Use the VibeComfy-enriched manifest as the source of expected model assets for selected templates.
2. Report missing assets with asset name, category/path, local paths checked through `extra_model_paths.yaml`, and known remediation when available.
3. Keep check non-mutating by default; it can print the exact reconcile command using the enriched manifest.
4. Ensure reconcile receives `--asset-manifest` or an enriched `--targets-json`; it should not attempt to derive Reigh route context or VibeComfy asset metadata locally.

## Phase 3: Shared Probes And CLI Surface

### Step 8: Build reusable in-pod health probes (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Refactor `verify_extracted_env()` into a shared function that accepts an already-connected SSH session, `PrebuiltEnvContract`, manifest, and optional enriched target/asset manifest data.
2. Probe the actual remote paths separately: `contract.runtime_venv_path` for worker and `contract.runtime_vibecomfy_path/.venv` for VibeComfy.
3. Check mount readability, required system packages (`ffmpeg`, `zstd`, `pv`), VibeComfy import, ComfyUI import, worker Torch/CUDA, VibeComfy Torch/CUDA, SageAttention only for `attention_profile=sage`, custom-node lock restore/list sanity, schema provider availability, `extra_model_paths.yaml`, selected source mode, selected node schemas, and selected model assets.
4. Return structured issue objects grouped by `environment`, `custom_nodes`, `workflow_source`, `schema`, `assets`, and `runtime_deferred`.

### Step 9: Add prebuilt check/status/cleanup/reconcile parser and dispatch (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Update the prebuilt dispatch map to include `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile` in addition to `build`, `inspect`, `invalidate`, and `list`.
2. Add parser entries and help text for all new subcommands.
3. Require `--dry-run` support for `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile`; dry-run must not require `RUNPOD_API_KEY` or provision a pod.
4. Add `--targets-json` and `--asset-manifest` for check/reconcile. Do not add Reigh-owned `--case` or `--route-key` flags to `runpod-lifecycle`.
5. Add `--json`, `--attention-profile portable|sage`, `--gpu-type`, `--container-disk-gb`, and `--keep-pod` where applicable.

### Step 10: Implement standalone check and reconcile with clean package boundaries (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. `rl prebuilt check` provisions a clearly named short-lived probe pod, attaches the selected volume, reads the manifest, extracts or inspects bundles as needed, runs the VibeComfy enrichment command remotely when a plain target manifest is supplied, runs shared in-pod probes, writes `env.health.json`, prints a concise result, and terminates by default.
2. `rl prebuilt reconcile` accepts an enriched asset manifest or runs remote VibeComfy enrichment first, then runs remote VibeComfy fetch/stage commands inside the pod checkout or stages known URLs directly into the model cache.
3. Neither command imports Reigh routing code or VibeComfy Python modules locally.
4. `--dry-run` for reconcile prints planned asset fetches and destinations without credentials or network calls.
5. `--keep-pod` is explicit and includes pod id/reason in output when used.

### Step 11: Add prebuilt status and cleanup safety (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/guard.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `rl prebuilt status --dry-run --json` that only lists pods matching explicit allowlisted prefixes: `reigh-livetest-builder-` and `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-`.
2. Add `rl prebuilt cleanup --older-than ... --dry-run --yes` that only terminates allowlisted prebuilt validation pods and never generic user pods.
3. Reuse existing prefix-pruning logic where practical, but keep the prebuilt allowlist visible and unit-tested.

## Phase 4: Live-Test Integration And Reports

### Step 12: Validate the actual consumer pod before queueing (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. After manifest read, hard-fail drift checks, extraction, worker git sync, VibeComfy git sync/delta install, and `_write_extra_model_paths_yaml()`, call the shared in-pod health probe directly on that same SSH session.
2. Generate the Reigh target manifest first, then run VibeComfy enrichment via the remote VibeComfy Python/checkout on the consumer pod before asset/schema checks.
3. Do not shell out to `rl prebuilt check` from inside `variant_prebuilt`; the CLI validates a separate probe pod and is only for standalone volume checks.
4. Abort before `register_worker_record()`, `launch_worker`, or `queue_matrix()` when health checks find environment, pure-Python source, schema, required input/connection, or asset failures.
5. Write the health summary, Reigh target manifest, and enriched VibeComfy manifest to the live-test run directory even on early failure when possible.

### Step 13: Add optional report metadata without breaking other variants (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/report.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, fresh/update call sites)
**Scope:** Small
1. Change `write_report(results, variant, pod_id, out_dir)` to accept an optional keyword-only metadata parameter, for example `write_report(..., metadata=None)`.
2. Have prebuilt pass volume id/name, data center, manifest path/hash, health report path/hash, target manifest path/hash, enriched manifest path/hash, selected profile, selected templates, GPU type, and check summary.
3. Leave fresh/update callers valid with no changes beyond the compatible function signature.

## Phase 5: Tests And Documentation

### Step 14: Add focused tests for builder, contracts, and CLI (`runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_cli.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_dry_run_smoke.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Test that the builder sync command pins the requested worker Python version and fails before manifest write on observed mismatch.
2. Test health report JSON round trip, separate worker/VibeComfy interpreter records, missing asset diagnostics, and schema-check issue grouping.
3. Update existing prebuilt help tests so they expect all subcommands: `build`, `inspect`, `invalidate`, `list`, `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile`.
4. Add no-credential dry-run tests for `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile`.
5. Add deterministic volume-selection tests for explicit volume name, explicit data center, multiple matching candidates, and canonical normalized names.

### Step 15: Add VibeComfy and live-test boundary tests (`vibecomfy/tests/...`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_variant_prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Test `ready_template_source_info()` or equivalent on pure Python templates, API-dict wrapper templates, and JSON reference/corpus paths.
2. Test the VibeComfy enrichment CLI: target manifest in, enriched source/schema/asset manifest out.
3. Test that Reigh target manifest generation resolves selected live-test cases/routes and excludes unrelated ready templates without importing `vibecomfy`.
4. Add a static/import-boundary test that fails if `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test` directly imports `vibecomfy` for target or asset manifest generation.
5. Test that prebuilt health failure occurs before worker registration, launch, and matrix queueing.
6. Test that optional report metadata preserves existing fresh/update report behavior.
7. Test that `runpod-lifecycle reconcile` consumes resolved/enriched metadata and does not parse Reigh `--case` or `--route-key` flags.

### Step 16: Document the operational workflow (`runpod-lifecycle/README.md`, nearest `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test` docs, relevant VibeComfy docs)
**Scope:** Small
1. Document prebuilt vs fresh, default portable RTX 4090 behavior, optional larger GPU and SageAttention profiles, and canonical volume naming from RunPod `data_center_id` normalization.
2. Document `build`, `check`, `reconcile`, `status`, `cleanup`, `invalidate`, and `list`, including dry-run/no-credential behavior and the `--targets-json`/`--asset-manifest` handoff.
3. Document that Reigh writes selected target metadata, VibeComfy enriches it, and RunPod lifecycle tooling consumes the JSON artifacts.
4. Document workflow onboarding: derive/convert/fork pure Python workflow, validate selected route/template schemas, verify assets, run cheap prebuilt check, run `z_image_turbo` smoke, then broaden to image/edit/upscale or video matrices.
5. State that app-active runtime workflow source must be pure Python; JSON/API-dict wrappers are allowed only as reference/import/corpus material.

## Phase 6: Validation

### Step 17: Run local verification
**Scope:** Small
1. Run targeted tests first: `pytest runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_cli.py reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_variant_prebuilt.py reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_dry_run_smoke.py` plus the VibeComfy source/enrichment tests.
2. Run broader affected suites after targeted tests pass.

### Step 18: Battle-test on RunPod
**Scope:** Large
1. Discover the actual current portable prebuilt volume and data center from RunPod instead of hard-coding the historical example.
2. Run `rl prebuilt check --targets-json <reigh-targets.json>` against the selected portable 4090 profile and confirm VibeComfy enrichment plus `env.health.json` evidence are written.
3. Run the first remote smoke gate with `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py --variant prebuilt --backend vibecomfy --case z_image_turbo` using the selected volume/profile.
4. Confirm pods launched by check/smoke are terminated unless intentionally preserved with `--keep-pod`, and record any preserved pod id and reason.
5. After base health and smoke pass, run broader image/edit/upscale or video matrix validation as a follow-up gate, not as the first blocker.

## Execution Order
1. Enforce builder worker Python version before trusting or expanding manifest/health evidence.
2. Add deterministic volume selection before wiring new commands that depend on volume/profile names.
3. Add VibeComfy source-mode API and VibeComfy-owned enrichment CLI before asset reconciliation.
4. Add Reigh target-manifest generation without VibeComfy imports.
5. Add parser/dispatch/dry-run behavior for new CLI verbs with JSON handoff before remote implementations.
6. Wire remote VibeComfy enrichment and shared in-pod probes into `variant_prebuilt` after target metadata and probes are stable.
7. Add report metadata compatibility and docs after command/output shape is settled.

## Validation Order
1. Start with builder Python enforcement and dry-run/no-credential CLI tests.
2. Run unit tests for health report serialization, volume selection, source classification, VibeComfy enrichment, target-manifest handoff, asset diagnostics, import-boundary checks, and cleanup allowlists.
3. Run live-test integration tests proving early failure before queueing.
4. Run broader affected local tests.
5. Run real `rl prebuilt check --targets-json ...` on RunPod.
6. Run real `z_image_turbo` prebuilt smoke and verify report evidence plus pod cleanup.
